- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/verify_prered.test.ts "FC-FULLSUITE-ANSI-IDS" (colored vitest transcript), loki-ts/tests/engine10/monorepo_cwd.test.ts (colored failing package run keeps ids and a redacted excerpt; child runs under FORCE_COLOR=0 NO_COLOR=1), loki-ts/tests/runner/harness_intelligence.test.ts (colored head excused against a plain superset baseline, colored new ids still block, unparseable ids named, excerpt in the brief). Red before the fix, green after.

## FC-WALL-OUT-OF-REPO-TARGET A Wall check resolved the code under test from an installed copy outside the repo, so the implementer edited runner config and the run ended PARTIAL (WALL-OUT-OF-REPO-TARGET)
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki start anthropics/claude-code#81826` on loki 11.17.76 ended PARTIAL with a runner-config tampering flag. Raw: the Wall test's target was `process.env.X ?? join(homedir(), '.claude/plugins/.../name')`, an installed copy, while the change lives in the repo; the implementer added bunfig.toml and a preload to redirect the env var.
- Law: a Wall check exercises the repo's own code on the tree under change; it never needs runner config to reach it.
- Cause (measured): the Wall writer prompt never said to use repo-relative paths and no validation looked at where a Wall file resolves its target.
- Siblings swept: Wall author (fixed); implement brief repro-test line (implementer-written tests are not statically checked; with an in-repo Wall the implementer has no reason to touch runner config); fix and plan stages write no tests.
- Mechanism: one `outOfRepoTarget` detector in project_model/conventions.ts (homedir, Path.home, $HOME, ~/; comments ignored; a literal absolute path with no home-derived value does not flag, TEN-QUALITY-41; the HOME-assignment exemption covers only lines from the assignment on, TEN-QUALITY-42); wallAuthor re-asks once with the precise reason, a file still failing is discarded (sealed copy only, listed in `discarded`); one sentence in the cache-stable Wall brief.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/fc23_wall_conventions.test.ts describe "FC-WALL-OUT-OF-REPO-TARGET" (rejected, re-asked, discarded; repo-relative and tmpdir pass). Red before the fix, green after. The detector also flags a quoted absolute home literal (/Users, /home, /root, C:\Users) that is the direct target of an import, require or source line (the installed-copy import shape), while plain string literals stay unflagged; fixture "literal import target flagged".
- Sibling (TEN-QUALITY-44, fixed): the implement-stage repro test (LOKI_E10_REPRO=1, lean path). After the session, changed test-like files go through the same `outOfRepoTarget` (`outOfRepoTests` in project_model/conventions.ts) and one re-ask built by the shared `outOfRepoReask` text the Wall also uses. Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/implement.test.ts `-t "repro test out of repo"`.
## FC-OUT-OF-REPO A request whose change has no target in the repository ran every stage before ending BLOCKED (OUT-OF-REPO-INTAKE)
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki start anthropics/claude-code#21527` (a feature whose code lives in a hosted Slack/web service) on 11.17.60 to 11.17.74 spent plan, wall, implement and fix before ending BLOCKED. Raw: the plan session had nothing to name, but plan-scope.json with zero files was read as "no scope", so the flow continued into implement.
- Law: cheap checks run before expensive stages; a run that cannot succeed says so at the first stage that knows; BLOCKED names the question, never a bare failure.
- Siblings swept: intake already_satisfied (short-circuits to the tail, unchanged), intent_declined (returns before wall/implement), a missing or malformed plan-scope.json (unknown, deliberately NOT out-of-repo), a skipped plan on small tasks (no signal, implementer still decides).
- Mechanism: one reader `planOutOfRepo` (util/run_cap.ts) fires only when plan-scope.json parses and names zero files, taking the planner's `out_of_repo` sentence as the reason; plan.ts records `out_of_repo` plus `spec_conflict_reason: "out-of-repo: <reason>"`; machine.ts seeds implement.exit = spec_conflict and jumps to the tail, so seal, outcomeOf, EXIT (4), the BLOCKED question comment and the receipt reuse the existing spec-conflict path (no new outcome). conflictReasonOf also reads the plan stage.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/plan.test.ts "FC-OUT-OF-REPO" (named files never fire; missing or malformed scope never fires) and loki-ts/tests/engine10/machine.test.ts "FC-OUT-OF-REPO machine" (implement, verify and fix do not run; positive control runs them). Red before the fix, green after.
## FC-NO-PUSH-UNVERIFIED A run that did not end verified pushed its loki/e10-* branch to the user's remote (NO-PUSH-UNVERIFIED)
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. BLOCKED runs of anthropics/claude-code#21527 pushed loki/e10-* branches and opened draft PRs on the user's fork asklokesh/claude-code. Raw: supervisor.ts gated the pr step on `verdict !== "ALREADY_SATISFIED" && hasDiff`, so FAILED, PARTIAL and SPEC_CONFLICT with any diff left the machine.
- Law: only a verified success leaves the machine; unverified work stays local as evidence.
- Siblings swept: the supervisor pr step and modernize/ship.ts shipWave (which pushed draft PRs for unverified waves; now refuses unless the wave verified and every unit is PROVEN) are the callers of the push scripts (engine10-push.sh, engine10-push-gitlab.sh); ALREADY_SATISFIED already opened none; the E-67 backstop commit still lands locally on a killed worker; cancelled and tampered runs already skip it.
- Mechanism: one gate in supervisor.ts, `verdict === "VERIFIED"`; every other verdict with a diff prints "branch loki/<runId> stays local (not pushed)" with the repo path. Supersedes the A-110 and E-67 draft-PR-on-unverified behaviour (tests updated in the same change).
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/supervisor_backstop.test.ts "FC-NO-PUSH-UNVERIFIED" (SPEC_CONFLICT, PARTIAL, FAILED call pr 0 times; VERIFIED once) and loki-ts/tests/engine10/modernize/ship.test.ts "FC-NO-PUSH-UNVERIFIED" (unverified wave pushes 0 times). Red before the fix, green after.
- Moat fixture: tests/moat/p9-rule-of-two.sh seeds a real pytest test and a project .venv (python and ruff shims) so its default, auto-pr and opt-out legs seal VERIFIED and Loki's own push and PR fire; its "unverified" leg removes the ruff shim, seals PARTIAL, and asserts "stays local (not pushed)" with no Loki push or PR.

## FC-LINT-PRE-RED: a lint check excused as failing on base still named the run's failure
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. A run whose only failing check was a lint check also failing on base (cc#81826, shellcheck SC2181 on both) printed `Reason: lint:shellcheck failed` and listed it under Failures in HANDOFF.md, so the founder read a base-relative pass as a blocker.
- Law: a check verify excused as pre-existing (`pre_red_checks`, TEN-QUALITY-03) is never the stated reason, never a Failure; it is shown separately as already failing on base.
- Cause (measured from .loki/runs events): verify judged lint base-relative correctly in all three rounds (`pre_red_checks: ["lint:shellcheck"]`, fix briefs excluded it, seal skipped it); only `reasonOf` (engine10/output.ts) and the handoff renderer (util/run_summary.ts) re-read raw `checks[].result` and ignored `pre_red_checks`.
- Siblings swept: seal verdict (already honours pre_red_checks), failures_grouped and fix brief (already exclude), reasonOf and handoff Failures (fixed); commands/export_sarif.ts receiptFindings (reads pre_red_checks from the run events), util/failure_memory.ts failedCheckNames (engine10/supervisor.ts now passes verify's pre_red_checks), e10ext/select.ts lintFails via AttemptCandidate.preRedChecks (all fixed through the one `isBlockingFail` in util/failure_memory.ts). features/attempts.ts now fills `preRedChecks` through the optional `AttemptOps.preRed(i)`, but stays inert until `score()`'s caller supplies preRed (the real treeswapOps runs no lint). Open: supervisor.ts must pass verify `pre_red_checks` as failedCheckNames' second argument. Other linters share the same path (ruff, eslint via excuseLint; unparseable output stays fail-closed with the existing reason).
- Mechanism: `pre_red_checks` read in `reasonOf`; `composeHandoff` tags failing checks in it `pre_red`, `renderHandoff` moves them to "Already failing on base".
- Fixture: tests/engine10/output.test.ts and tests/util/run_summary.test.ts, "FC-LINT-PRE-RED". Red before the fix, green after.

## FC-SEAL-NA-AS-UNPROVEN A package with no test command, a repo with no app, and deep checks the risk tier never asked for were listed NOT PROVEN, so a fully proven run ended PARTIAL or carried phantom gaps (SEAL-NA-AS-UNPROVEN)
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki start anthropics/claude-code#81826` on loki 11.17.78 (run e10-20261019T064138Z-a7ca) ended PARTIAL solely because verify.not_proven held "full suite: package claude-code-repo defines no test command", while the full-suite runs for the other packages and the Wall test passed. Raw: a package without a test command has nothing to run; it is not applicable, not unproven. Every receipt also listed "full suite", "app boot", "council" and "security scan" as NOT PROVEN even when the risk tier never selected them or verify had run the full suite.
- Law: NOT PROVEN lists what was needed and not shown; what does not apply is disclosed separately and never hidden; unknown stays unproven (fail-safe); a real gap never becomes VERIFIED.
- Cause (measured): project_model/package_suite.ts added the no-test-command note unconditionally; util/app_probe.ts returned not_run when app-runner reported "no application detected"; seal.ts spread the static DEEP_NOT_PROVEN constant into every receipt without reading risk.checks_deferred. The only N/A filter was a prefix match on "full suite (no test runner" in verify.ts.
- Siblings swept: package_suite.ts runPackageSuites (fixed); deep.ts runFullSuite legacy "full suite (no test runner detected)" (now N/A, listed); verify.ts not_proven filter (one naNote helper replaces the prefix match); app_probe.ts "app boot" and "http probe" (n/a only when app-runner itself said no application detected; a Docker project with the daemon down, an unsafe command or a throw stay not_run); deep.ts runAppBoot and the addendum not_proven; seal.ts DEEP_NOT_PROVEN (now deepGaps from risk.checks_selected and checks_deferred, fail-safe to the full list when risk is absent or malformed); supply, review, contract and wall not_proven are real gaps and untouched.
- Mechanism: one `naNote`/`naText` pair in package_suite.ts (the "n/a: " prefix travels in the existing notProven Set) and one `deepGaps` in util/check_result.ts. A package without tests is N/A only when a real suite ran AND (the diff is not in that package OR a passing Wall or holdout check whose test file lies inside THAT package proves it: the package root owns the file, root "." owns any file inside no other package, and a Wall that failed anywhere voids the proof; round 2 closed the cross-package leak); an unknown diff, or changed files it owns with no Wall proof, stay NOT PROVEN. N/A notes reach the receipt as the additive `not_applicable` field and a "### N/A" section; they never enter verdictOf. Tier-skipped deep checks read "not selected by risk tier: X" there; a selected-but-unrun check stays NOT PROVEN; an executed full suite is dropped.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/monorepo_cwd.test.ts (package without tests: N/A, owned-by-diff stays NOT PROVEN with and without proofFiles, an in-package proof makes it N/A, a proof in another package does not, root package with plugins/** diff stays N/A, unknown diff fail-safe; verify wiring describe mutates proofFiles, wallProofOf and the not_proven filter), seal.test.ts describe "FC-SEAL-NA-AS-UNPROVEN" (N/A section, low and high tier, executed full suite dropped, no-risk fail-safe, real gap still downgrades), tests/util/check_result.test.ts deepGaps, tests/util/app_probe.test.ts (empty repo n/a, missing Docker runtime stays not_run), deep.test.ts (n/a boot, no runner). Red before the fix, green after.

## FC-WALL-PROOF-PACKAGE-BLIND A passing Wall at the repo root that drives a test-less package's changed files by path never credited that package, so a multi-root run sealed PARTIAL instead of VERIFIED
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki start anthropics/claude-code#81826` on loki 11.17.97 sealed PARTIAL: the diff changed plugins/ralph-wiggum/hooks/stop-hook.sh and scripts/setup-ralph-loop.sh, the Project Model (LLM generated, nondeterministic) declared a package `plugins` with no test command, and the Wall (always at the repo root since 11.17.95, red on base, green on head, driving both scripts by path) never matched it. The note "full suite: package plugins defines no test command" stayed NOT PROVEN, the verdict went PARTIAL before crossReview ran (xreview.ts only reviews VERIFIED), and "one reviewer" was also reported NOT PROVEN. The previous run, whose model had no `plugins` package, sealed VERIFIED. Raw: proof credit was decided by where the proof file lives, not by what it exercises.
- Law: a passing proof proves what it exercises; credit follows the code under test, never the proof's location or the model's nondeterministic package split. Unknown stays unproven (fail-safe).
- Cause (measured): runPackageSuites credited a test-less package only when a proof file's packageOf(...).root equalled the package root.
- Siblings swept: package_suite.ts runPackageSuites is the only consumer of proofFiles for package credit (grep proofFiles, packageOf over loki-ts/src: deep.ts and verify.ts only forward the list; verify.ts wallProofOf only decides whether the Wall passed; seal.ts and check_result.ts deepGaps consume the resulting note prefix, not the proof). No other place credits proof by package root or location, so there is nothing else to fix.
- Mechanism: execution evidence, never proof text (a text or path match is gameable, and the real Wall locates scripts by basename search, never naming a path). One `execProves` plus `wallRedOnBaseOf` in package_suite.ts, fed by verify.ts through the existing runFullSuite opts (`wallBaseRed` from wall.base_run). A test-less package is N/A when (a) the Wall was red on base (base_run.fail > 0; missing, all not_run or green never credits), (b) it passed on head (proofFiles non-empty from wallProofOf, which already needs every Wall and holdout check to pass), and (c) the diff is known, non-empty after dropping this run's own Wall files, has no quoted path, and every remaining file is owned by that package. A red-on-base, green-on-head Wall over a diff confined to one package necessarily exercised that package's change. A diff spanning packages gets no credit from this rule; the in-package proof rule is kept.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/monorepo_cwd.test.ts describe "FC-WALL-PROOF-PACKAGE-BLIND" (credited with base red and a single-package diff; NOT PROVEN with base green, base_run missing, a two-package diff, unknown, empty or quoted diff, Wall failed on head; wallRedOnBaseOf cases). Red before the fix, green after.

## FC-WALL-FILE-OWNS-EVERY-PACKAGE The run's own passing Wall file at the repo root has no owning package, so every test-less package read as owning the diff and stayed NOT PROVEN
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki start anthropics/claude-code#81826` on loki 11.17.99 sealed PARTIAL with "full suite: package scripts defines no test command" although the whole diff was under plugins/ (package `plugins`, credited by FC-WALL-PROOF-PACKAGE-BLIND) and `scripts` was untouched. Run e10-20261019T165943Z-a552: wall base_run fail=1, Wall green on head.
- Law: a proof is not product. Ownership of a diff is decided by the product files it changes; an unknown, quoted or unowned product path still fails safe to owned.
- Cause (measured): runPackageSuites `owns` counted changed files with no owning package as owned by every package; tests/loki_wall_*.test.ts (the root Wall) is such a file. execProves already excluded the Wall files; `owns` did not.
- Siblings swept: execProves (already excludes Wall files), the proofFiles credit path (unchanged), and the quoted-path and unresolved-path rows of FC-CHANGED-PATH-QUOTED (still owned: only this run's passing Wall files, from proofFiles, are excluded).
- Mechanism: one `product` list in runPackageSuites, the changed files minus this run's passing Wall files (the same set execProves excludes). Unknown diff or a Wall-only diff stays owned (fail-safe). A failed Wall has no proofFiles, so nothing is excluded.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/monorepo_cwd.test.ts test "FC-WALL-FILE-OWNS-EVERY-PACKAGE" (N/A with a passing root Wall and an untouched test-less package; NOT PROVEN when the Wall failed or an unowned product path is present). Red on HEAD 2739c8e26 (1 fail), green after (38/0).

## FC-VERIFIED-PR-WITHOUT-ITS-TEST A VERIFIED run's pushed branch never contained the acceptance test that made it VERIFIED
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. anthropics/claude-code#81826, run e10-20261019T171807Z-7698, sealed VERIFIED; its commit changed 2 product files and tests/loki_wall_ralph_max_iterations.test.ts stayed untracked, so the PR reviewer saw "Missing tests: the diff has no test changes" for the very test that ran green.
- Law: a proof a reviewer is asked to trust must be reproducible from the pushed branch (evidence or it did not happen). Not product, but publishable once the verdict is sealed.
- Cause (measured): commit_filter.ts dropSet drops every WALL_FILE (loki_wall_*) in the commit stage and in backstopCommit, and nothing publishes the Wall afterwards, even on VERIFIED.
- Siblings swept: backstopCommit (killed or crashed worker: stays dropped), discardIfSatisfied, runPackageSuites proofFiles (FC-WALL-FILE-OWNS-EVERY-PACKAGE), flagOutsideScope, formatChanged, receipt diff_sha256 and head_sha, seal_drift head check, reviewer_body changed-files list: none sees a Wall file as product, because the Wall is published in a SEPARATE child commit made at seal after the verdict.
- Mechanism: publishableWall (util/check_result.ts) selects the Wall files verify ran and passed (target_checks + executed proof), reads the SEALED copy under runDir/wall and requires it to hash to wall.files[].sha256 (mismatch: not published, NOT PROVEN note). The seal stage, only when the verdict is VERIFIED, writes the sealed bytes and makes one child commit (trailer Loki-Wall-Publish). The receipt binds the PRODUCT head (head_sha, tree, diff_sha256 computed before the child commit); the pushed head is its child whose only change is Wall files, so sealDrift reads it as benign head movement (no overlap with the sealed diff). The PR body gains an "Acceptance tests" section (paths and the exact verify command). Opt-out LOKI_COMMIT_WALL=0.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/seal.test.ts describe "FC-VERIFIED-PR-WITHOUT-ITS-TEST" and loki-ts/tests/engine10/pr_body_golden.test.ts (publish on VERIFIED, sealed bytes not agent bytes, failed/not-run/PARTIAL/opt-out/backstop keep dropping, receipt head and diff unchanged, PR body section). Red on base 4ade2c9a6, green after.

## FC-CHANGED-PATH-QUOTED A non-ASCII changed path came back from git as a quoted octal escape, so no consumer of changedFiles could match it to a package, Wall file or test
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. A diff in `frontend/src/café.ts` made the test-less frontend package read as not owned by the diff, its note became N/A, not_proven was empty and the run could seal VERIFIED with the frontend edit unproven (HIGH re-review of FC-SEAL-NA-AS-UNPROVEN r2).
- Law broken: a git path must be the real path; an unresolvable path must fail safe, never read as "not in the package".
- Siblings swept: every consumer of changedFiles/diffFiles (package ownership, uncoveredChanged, risk tier, impacted tests, lint selection); diffFiles numstat and name-status keyed by the same path (fixed together); secure_scan.ts already used quotePath=false.
- Mechanism: `changedFiles` uses `-c core.quotePath=false` and `-z` (NUL split) for both git calls, diffFiles numstat and name-status use `-z` with `--no-renames` (simple NUL records, so a name holding a quote, backslash, tab or newline is never escaped); `--no-renames` on the changedFiles diff too, because a rename listed only its new path and hid the source package; the package ownership check treats a changed path packageOf cannot resolve, or one that starts with a quote, as owned (stays NOT PROVEN).
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/util/check_result.test.ts and tests/engine10/monorepo_cwd.test.ts, "FC-CHANGED-PATH-QUOTED" (non-ASCII path in changedFiles; git mv across packages keeps the source path; diffFiles on a name with a double quote; verify with a non-ASCII frontend file and a backend Wall stays NOT PROVEN; unresolvable path fail-safe). Red before the fix, green after.

## FC-WALL-PHANTOM-PATH A Wall check located the code under test at a repo path that does not exist, and the implementer added a symlink to make it resolve (PR carried a link instead of the change)
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki start anthropics/claude-code#81826` on loki 11.17.77: the issue names the plugin "ralph-loop", the repo directory is plugins/ralph-wiggum. Raw: the Wall wrote `join(ROOT, 'plugins', 'ralph-loop')` (absent) and the implementer committed `plugins/ralph-loop -> ralph-wiggum` to satisfy it.
- Law: a Wall check names code that exists in the base tree or that the plan creates; a change never satisfies a check by adding an alias for a path.
- Cause (measured): FC-WALL-OUT-OF-REPO-TARGET validated only home-derived targets; nothing resolved repo-relative literals against the base tree, and seal looked at no added-symlink signal (E-55 covers only base-side typechanges of test files).
- Siblings swept: Wall author (fixed); implement stage (nothing to forbid in-brief; the diff is judged at seal); fix and plan stages write no Wall tests; seal diff checks (weakened test, runner config) did not look at mode 120000 on the head side (fixed).
- Mechanism: `phantomRepoPaths` in project_model/conventions.ts evaluates join/resolve chains rooted at the test dir or cwd (const assignments followed) and reports missing paths the plan does not name; wallAuthor folds it into the existing single re-ask (reason plus same-named files from the repo map) and discards a file still failing. The detector resolves process.cwd() against both the test's nearest package root and the repo root, and skips paths the test creates, git ignores, or the plan names WITH its parent (plugins/ralph-loop; never a bare segment or basename). `process.env.X ?? Y` and `|| Y` evaluate as Y (the real #81826 file used `process.env.REPO_ROOT ?? process.cwd()`); the two cwd runs pair calls by source line and text, not array index. `addedSymlinkNotes` (util/base_guard.ts, folded into seal's rcNotes) makes an added symlink (status A, or T from a regular file; never M) or added submodule pointer a NOT PROVEN item that blocks VERIFIED unless the plan asks for one by the word symlink or submodule, un-negated; alias is not an escape.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/fc23_wall_conventions.test.ts describe "FC-WALL-PHANTOM-PATH" (reported, existing passes, plan-created passes, re-ask, discard); loki-ts/tests/engine10/seal.test.ts "FC-WALL-PHANTOM-PATH" (added symlink, plan-requested, deletion, modified link, negated plan, submodule). Round 2 adds monorepo cwd, created-path, ignored-path, plan-parent, split-call and same-name-directory cases, and a fixture built from the real #81826 Wall file. Red before the fix, green after.
- Sibling note (in-test-dir phantom, v11.17.83, #81826 rerun): the Wall wrote `resolve(import.meta.dir, 'plugins/ralph-loop')`, which is tests/plugins/ralph-loop, absent from the base tree. Raw: phantomRepoPaths reported only paths escaping the test dir, so it passed; the implementer symlinked tests/plugins/ralph-loop -> ../../plugins/ralph-wiggum and seal ended PARTIAL.
- Law: same as above; a repo path the Wall invents is corrected up front, whether or not it escapes the test dir.
- Siblings swept: the escape-only assumption lived in one place (the report condition in `ev`); outOfRepoTarget, seal's addedSymlinkNotes and the implement repro-test path make no such assumption.
- Mechanism: `strayInTestDir` in phantomRepoPaths additionally reports a path that stays inside the test dir when its first segment is absent from the test dir but present at the package or repo root (the author meant a repo path). Same exemptions (plan with parent, created, ignored by the VCS, write/expect/tmp lines); fixtures the test dir lacks (fixtures/a.json) and __self__ stay unreported.
- Fixture: fc23_wall_conventions.test.ts "in-testdir phantom" (incident lines report tests/plugins/ralph-loop; plan-named, fixtures, created and __self__ paths pass). Red before the fix, green after.
- Siblings swept (review of 11.17.85): the package-root fallback trusts a known project model fully (its package cwd, else the repo root) and otherwise walks up to the nearest manifest-shaped JSON (string name plus another key); config files (tsconfig, linter, test-runner, xml, cfg, toml) are never roots, so they cannot hide a real phantom (fixture "fallback no false root"). Known limit: a nameless manifest or a non-JSON-manifest package resolves only through the model; without one the repo root is the cwd. The vendor-dir exemption is the ignore check alone, with no vendor-dir name or `*_modules` pattern, so src/my_modules/x and src/feature_modules/x are reported (fixture "modules dir phantom"). The release-gate pytest guard strips only the pip install segment before checking (tests/test-release-gate-dedup.sh M10).
- Sibling note (walk-up base, run e10-20261019T121849Z-848d, #81826 rerun): the Wall found the plugin with `for (const start of [process.cwd(), import.meta.dir]) { let dir = resolve(start); while (true) { const candidate = join(dir, 'plugins', 'ralph-loop'); ... dir = dirname(dir) } }` and threw 'plugins/ralph-loop not found'; the repo has plugins/ralph-wiggum. Raw: the loop variable is unresolved, so phantomRepoPaths evaluated nothing, no re-ask ran, the implementer could not touch the Wall test and the run sealed SPEC_CONFLICT.
- Law: same as above; a repo path the Wall invents is corrected up front, however the base directory is reached.
- Siblings swept: the only place that dropped unresolved bases is `ev` in phantomRepoPaths; outOfRepoTarget and seal's addedSymlinkNotes build no paths. Error-message literals like 'plugins/ralph-loop' are not path construction and are never evidence.
- Mechanism: in phantomRepoPaths a `for (const X of [items])` makes X, and `let dir = resolve(X)`, a walk-up base only when every item evaluates to the cwd or the test directory (process.cwd(), import.meta.dir, __dirname or a const bound to them); repo-relative strings and join(...) items leave X unresolved, as before. A join/resolve on a walk-up base with literal segments is reported when its first literal segment exists at the package or repo root and the full path is missing at every directory the walk passes through: the test dir, the package root and the repo root, and each parent between them and the repo root. A path found at any of those directories is real and is not reported. A first segment absent everywhere stays silent as before; paths built on a walk-up base never use the escape rule, and nested joins on a candidate are not double reported. Temp and mkdtemp bases never resolve and stay unflagged; plan, created and ignored exemptions are unchanged.
- Fixture: fc23_wall_conventions.test.ts "walk-up phantom" (the real Wall shape reports plugins/ralph-loop; ralph-wiggum, plan-named, absent first segment, mkdtemp and tmpdir bases give []; HIGH review block fixtures FP-A repo-relative string loop, FP-B join on cwd loop, FP-C several joins loop, FP-D2 walk-up whose file exists on the way up all give []). Red before the fix, green after.

## FC-MEDIUM-REVIEWER-UNCREDITED The medium tier selects "one reviewer", nothing on the default path runs or credits it, so every medium run lists it NOT PROVEN and seals PARTIAL
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. A real run on anthropics/claude-code#81826 (v11.17.83, risk tier medium) listed "one reviewer" under NOT PROVEN.
- Law broken: a check the tier selected is credited when, and only when, it really ran (evidence or it did not happen); a check that did not run stays NOT PROVEN and is never hidden or dropped from the plan.
- Cause (measured): check_result.ts PLAN.medium selects "one reviewer"; deferredOf keeps it in checks_deferred unless markExecuted names it; markExecuted has one caller (verify.ts, naming only "full test command" and "full suite"); no code names "one reviewer". The only reviewers are seal's crossReview (xreview.ts, opt-in via loki.yaml review, LOKI_REVIEW_PROVIDER or LOKI_XVENDOR_DEFAULT) and deep's council session (a detached worker that runs after seal). deepGaps (11.17.82, FC-SEAL-NA-AS-UNPROVEN) began pushing every deferred non-deep item into notProven; before it the receipt listed only the four static deep items, so this is a visibility regression of an old unkept promise, not a new bug in the reviewer.
- Siblings swept (every PLAN.selected item): "targeted tests" and "lint of changed files" run always (RAN_ALWAYS); "full test command" and "full suite" are credited by verify.ts markExecuted and deepGaps fullRan; "council" and "security scan" (high) are credited by deep checks via executed(); "one reviewer" had no credit path (fixed here). Low tier names are disclosed as not selected.
- Mechanism: one `REVIEWER_ITEM` credit rule inside `deepGaps`: credited when seal's cross-review returned a verdict (level not "not_run") or a deep council check executed. No review configured or a not_run review keeps it NOT PROVEN. The default path still has no reviewer, so default medium runs stay PARTIAL by design until a reviewer is configured; running a default same-vendor reviewer on every medium run is a separate cost decision.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/util/check_result.test.ts "medium reviewer" (tier alone, not_run review, verdict review, council pass and not_run, high council not credited by a cross-review, low unaffected). Red with the credit removed, green after.
- Sibling note (medium default review): the credit above was inert on a default run because the cross-review itself was opt-in (loki.yaml `review:`, LOKI_REVIEW_PROVIDER or LOKI_XVENDOR_DEFAULT). `crossReview` in engine10/stages/xreview.ts now defaults to the run's own provider and CLI (same vendor) when the risk record says medium and nothing is configured; a configured reviewer or council agent keeps precedence, and LOKI_REVIEW_PROVIDER=off or none opts out (one reviewer then stays NOT PROVEN). The receipt `review` block carries `same_vendor: true, defaulted: true`. A BLOCK still caps the verdict at PARTIAL through `minVerdict` (never upgrades). Low and high tiers do not default. Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/xreview.test.ts describe "medium default review" (red with the default removed). Slow-reviewer follow-up: seal (limit 60s) used to kill the stage, and the receipt, when a default review ran long; seal now gets `extraLimitS` (the review box, LOKI_E10_REVIEW_BOX_S, default 300s, plus 30s) only when a review is planned, the stage abort signal reaches the reviewer child, a review past its box is not_run, and the receipt review block carries `level`. Fixtures: seal.test.ts "slow reviewer keeps receipt" and "seal passes reviewRan through".

## FC-OUTCOME-FLOOD A per-item NOT PROVEN list (pre-red tests) had no cap, so one run printed about 300 "pre red:" entries and filled the terminal, PR body and receipt.md
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. Run e10-20261019T130749Z-f11c on asklokesh/FireLater#20 (11.17.93): every already-failing frontend test appeared twice on the NOT PROVEN line, once by short name and once by full path name, next to "baseline: 1 pre-existing failing check(s) (not blocking), 0 introduced".
- Law broken: a disclosure must stay readable; an informational count is one line, the detail lives in the machine record.
- Cause (measured): seal.ts adds one `pre red: <id>` per verify.pre_red id (verify records both name forms); output.ts, reviewer_body (via pr.ts), renderReceiptMd and the handoff each printed every receipt not_proven line unbounded.
- Siblings swept (every per-item list in seal's notProven): pre red, not run, flaky test (also the separate flaky clause in formatSummary), skipped on base (excused), changed untested after limit, reverted test edit, wall test discarded, Wall test not executed, weakened test. Single-fact lines (baseline, verdicts) are not lists and stay.
- Mechanism: one display-only `collapseNotProven` in util/term_width.ts, applied at the four surfaces. Pre-red names are deduplicated (a short name is the tail of its " > " full path twin) and collapse to "pre red: N tests already failing on base (listed in receipt.json)" past 3; other lists collapse to "<prefix>N entries (listed in receipt.json)" past 8. Idempotent. receipt.json not_proven and the receipt digest are unchanged.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/output.test.ts "collapseNotProven" (150 short plus full-name entries), route_receipt_golden.test.ts "FC-OUTCOME-FLOOD receipt.md", tests/util/run_summary.test.ts handoff case. Red before the helper existed, green after.

## FC-WALL-LOCATION-BLIND The Wall author was never told where its file would live, so a root-relative __dirname built phantom paths; and a stale untracked Wall file from an earlier run was credited as an exogenous pass
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki start asklokesh/FireLater#20` on loki 11.17.93 (run e10-20261019T130749Z-f11c) sealed PARTIAL with no Wall proof. Raw: the author wrote "this file lives at the repo root" with `const root = __dirname` and joined backend/src and frontend/src; Loki installs Wall files beside the first detected test (backend/src/lib), so the detector correctly flagged backend/src/lib/backend/src. The re-ask said "locate the real code by searching", which misdiagnosed a location error. Separately the receipt listed "wall test discarded: loki_wall_websocket.test.ts" and also an exogenous pass for vitest:backend/src/lib/loki_wall_websocket.test.ts.
- Law: an author that writes a file is told the repo-relative directory the file will run from and what __dirname resolves to there; a re-ask names the real cause; a discarded Wall file is never also credited as a check.
- Cause (measured): wallAuthor passed no destination to buildWallBrief (the brief says the author cannot see the repository); phantomReason had no notion of the test directory. The pass came from an untracked loki_wall_websocket.test.ts (mtime 00:55, an earlier run's installed Wall file, a different body than this run's discarded copy) still in the working tree: verify's changedFiles lists untracked files, so it ran as a changed test and lint target.
- Siblings swept: Wall author (fixed: brief names the destination, re-ask names the cause); implement bug-repro test and fix stage (an in-repo session whose cwd is the repo, so the file location is the author's own choice; nothing blind); verify changed set (fixed: a loki_wall_* path not installed by this run's Wall is dropped; the commit stage already excludes the prefix); holdout files are staged later by stageHoldout and unaffected.
- Mechanism: buildWallBrief takes the destination from wallTargetDir (the value installCore copies to); phantomReason(miss, repoFiles, testDir, pkgRoot) names "your test is copied to X/, __dirname is that directory, not the repo root; Y exists relative to the repo root" when the phantom is testDir joined with a path real at the repo or package root; util/check_result.ts withoutStaleWall filters verify's changed list.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/fc23_wall_conventions.test.ts describe "FC-WALL-LOCATION-BLIND" (brief, reason, end-to-end re-ask with the run's file shape, stale file excluded, own file kept). Red before the fix (4 of 6), green after.

## FC-XREVIEW-BLIND-TOOLS The seal cross-reviewer is given no shell and a prompt that asks it to check behaviour, so it reports "my tool output came back empty" as a review flag and reviews blind
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. Run e10-20261019T124856Z-0c36 (anthropics/claude-code#81826, 11.17.93) sealed with two NOT PROVEN cross-review flags: "My tool output came back empty, so I could not confirm..." and "Acceptance lines, traced by hand because the shell output was not visible".
- Law broken: a reviewer is told what it can and cannot do; a limit of its own sandbox is never reported to the user as a defect of the work (evidence or it did not happen).
- Cause (REAL ROOT CAUSE, corrected after HIGH review of 54ffc9f0c): `leanReviewerFlags()` emitted `--tools=`, which disables ALL tools in Claude CLI 2.1.303 (stream-json init shows `"tools":[]` for the old and the first-fix argv alike), so allowlist and denylist were moot and the Bash deny was a red herring. Original observation: reviewArgv for claude passed `--allowedTools Read,Grep,Glob --disallowedTools Write,Edit,NotebookEdit,Bash`, so the reviewer had no shell at all; the prompt (acceptance block: "Check the diff against each line") invites running commands. Repro with the exact flags (haiku, `a.sh` containing `echo hi`): "This session has no shell tool ... there is no bash output", permission_denials empty. The process-group spawn (11.17.92) and cwd/env are not involved; the reviewer ran and returned a verdict.
- Siblings swept: council voters (council/voter_agents.ts) and quality_gates reviewers already use the shared REVIEW_ALLOWLIST_TOKEN (read-only git/cat/ls/grep/rg/find/head/tail/wc) plus REVIEW_GUARD_DENYLIST (mutations only), so they can inspect; seal xreview was the one reviewer with a blanket Bash deny. Codex xreview runs in its read-only sandbox with a shell, unaffected.
- Mechanism: xreview claude argv passes `leanReviewerFlags({ tools: "Read,Grep,Glob,Bash" })` = `--tools=Read,Grep,Glob,Bash` (one argument, so the variadic option cannot swallow the prompt), Bash narrowed by the shared allowlist; the prompt (REVIEW_TOOLS_NOTE) lists only the tools the reviewer has and tells it to say so when it could not inspect something (it is never told to hide an inspection failure). Write escapes under the allowlist (reproduced: `rg --pre rm`, `git diff --output=f`) are closed in REVIEW_GUARD_DENYLIST (TS and autonomy/lib/claude-flags.sh, byte-identical): `rg --pre/--pre-glob`, `--output`/`--ext-diff`/`--no-index` on git diff/log/show/blame, `git --exec-path`, `find -delete/-exec/-execdir/-ok/-fprint/-fls`, each as a prefix form and a mid-argument glob form (`Bash(git * --output*)`; live probe confirmed `git diff HEAD~1 --output=pwned.txt` and `rg one --pre rm a.txt` are denied). This also hardens council voters and quality_gates, which share the list. Sibling sweep: `leanReviewerFlags` default (`--tools=`) is unchanged for its other caller, quality_gates claudeReviewer (haiku-routed text-only reviewer, no allowlist, diff in prompt; blind by design, not the bug); council voters do not use it. Live init line (haiku, real argv, stream-json): `"tools":["Bash","Glob","Grep","Read"]`. Round 2 (HIGH review of e4e690dce): allowed `git status/diff/show/log` execute programs named by the repo `.git/config` and attributes (core.fsmonitor, diff drivers, textconv) and so can rewrite the worktree before seal hashes it; xreview therefore uses `REVIEW_ALLOWLIST_NO_GIT` (shared allowlist minus every `Bash(git` entry; the diff is already in the prompt) and the note says no git commands are available. Repo settings: with `--setting-sources project` a repo `.claude/settings.json` can carry `permissions.allow` or `defaultMode`, and the machine user `defaultMode: auto` made `touch marker.txt` run in a live haiku probe (settings-source-dependent, not proven for a trusted repo offline), so xreview now passes `--setting-sources user` plus `--permission-mode dontAsk`; probe with those flags: `cat f.txt` ran, `touch marker.txt` was denied and no marker was created even with a repo settings file allowing touch and bypassPermissions. Round 3 correction: `--setting-sources user` was itself a hole (user `permissions.allow` such as Bash(python3:*) pre-approves under dontAsk, user hooks fire in every review child, and the user CLAUDE.md added about 4,331 cache-creation tokens per review). xreview now passes `--setting-sources ""` (no source loads; the CLI accepts the empty value, "none" is rejected): live probe with the real argv, `python3 -c "print(6*7)"` denied, `cat f.txt` ran, 0 hook events (vs 12 with `user`), init tools `["Bash","Glob","Grep","Read"]`. Also closed: `rg --hostname-bin` (runs a program). A leading `rg --pre=CMD` form slipped past both `Bash(rg --pre=:*)` (prefix rules need a word boundary) and `Bash(rg * --pre*)` (needs an argument before it); live probe confirmed it ran until `Bash(rg --pre*)` and `Bash(rg --hostname-bin*)` were added, then denied. The new test records the argv the stub reviewer CLI really receives. Sibling note: council voters and quality_gates share the git allowlist and are exposed to repo-config driver execution; follow-up slice FC-REVIEWER-GIT-CONFIG-EXEC (not fixed here). Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/xreview.test.ts describe "FC-XREVIEW-BLIND-TOOLS" (red before, green after).

## FC-PR-BODY-PUBLIC-HYGIENE The PR body, written for a public upstream repo, carried template boilerplate, a local absolute path, a file the commit never included, unlabeled base-red checks and flags cut mid-sentence
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. Run e10-20261019T124856Z-0c36 on anthropics/claude-code#81826 sealed VERIFIED, and its PR body said: "What the issue asked" listed the issue template's checklist ("I have searched existing issues..."); "What changed" listed tests/loki_wall_ralph_max_iterations.test.ts, a Wall file the commit stage drops; Receipt printed `File: /private/tmp/.../receipt.json`; "How it was tested" showed `shellcheck ... -> fail` with no context while the handoff section called the same check "Already failing on base"; NOT PROVEN bullets were cut at 300 characters ("The \"no state") and one bullet was a dangling header ("Acceptance lines, traced by hand ...:") whose sub-lines fell past the 5-note cap.
- Law: every line of a body that leaves the machine is true of the PR (only files in the commit, only what was asked), contains no local path, labels a check the same way everywhere it appears, and never shows half a sentence.
- Siblings swept: status-comment (receipt_surface.ts renderReceiptComment: prints digest and `loki verify <id>` only, no path, no file list, no check results; clean); HANDOFF.md (run_summary.ts renderHandoff already splits base-red checks under its own heading; its notProven clip ends with an ellipsis; clean); reviewer brief (`checks run: 4 (3 pass, 1 fail, ...)` hid base-red, and oneLine cut NOT PROVEN with no ellipsis: fixed); receipt.md renderer (`- fail: lint:shellcheck` hid base-red: fixed).
- Mechanism: one label helper in util/check_result.ts (`checkResultLabel`, `ALREADY_FAILING_ON_BASE`) used by the body builder, the brief and receipt.md; the receipt check gains an optional additive `pre_red: true` (absent on older receipts, digest computation untouched) and older runs fall back to verify.pre_red_checks. `renderReviewerBody` drops Wall files from the file list, strips issue-template checklist lines (preferring the intent card's "What I think you want" line), and prints the receipt path repo-relative. `parseReview` keeps each reviewer flag whole (header lines absorb their sub-lines, a header with nothing under it is dropped, cap 600 characters with an ellipsis).
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/reviewer_body.test.ts "FC-PR-BODY-PUBLIC-HYGIENE" (real #81826 shape: template body, excluded Wall file, base-failing shellcheck, absolute receipt path); xreview.test.ts "parseReview keeps flags whole"; reviewer_brief.test.ts and seal.test.ts base-red labels. Red before the fix, green after.

## FC-CP-DENYLIST-DRIFT The control-plane Ask policy kept a hand-copied literal of REVIEW_GUARD_DENYLIST, so growing the loki-ts list (11.17.96 xreview rg --pre, git --exec-path, find -exec forms) turned main CI Tests red
- User-visible vs raw: raw (CI only). packages/control-plane/test/server/ask_policy.test.ts "the deny list covers every token of the loki-ts REVIEW denylist" failed with `Expected to contain: "Bash(git --exec-path:*)"` on main from v11.17.96. No user impact: the Ask job already denies the blanket `Bash`, so the missing tokens were redundant there; the guard exists so the mirror never silently lags.
- Law broken: a mirrored security list is either derived from its source or guarded by a parity test that fails in the same slice that edits the source (a release that adds tokens must update every mirror before it ships).
- Siblings swept: DENIED_TOOLS in packages/control-plane/src/ask/policy.ts (the only copy that was stale; fixed); autonomy/lib/claude-flags.sh (byte-identical mirror, already updated in 11.17.96); loki-ts consumers (stages/xreview.ts, stages/deep.ts, runner/quality_gates.ts, council) import the exported constant, no copy; no other mirror of REVIEW_GUARD_DENYLIST or REVIEW_ALLOWLIST_NO_GIT exists under packages/ or tests/ (grep of the whole tree excluding node_modules and dist). tests/test-control-plane.sh (CP-04) runs the control-plane tests as one bun process, so it was red from this same drift and no other cause.
- Mechanism: the control-plane package ships alone (Docker image has no loki-ts), so a cross-package import is not allowed; DENIED_TOOLS keeps a literal copy of every REVIEW_GUARD_DENYLIST token and the parity test now asserts `missing` is empty, so a failure lists every absent token at once instead of the first.
- Fixture: packages/control-plane/test/server/ask_policy.test.ts "the deny list covers every token of the loki-ts REVIEW denylist". Red before (missing "Bash(git --exec-path:*)" and the later tokens), green after.

## FC-REVIEWER-GIT-CONFIG-EXEC A reviewer child with the shared Bash allowlist (git status, diff, show, log) runs programs named in the target repo's own git config
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible as integrity, not output. A HIGH reviewer reproduced it: a plain `git status` in a reviewer child executed the repo's core.fsmonitor script and rewrote `.loki/runs/<id>/events.jsonl`; `git diff` did the same through diff.external and diff.<driver>.command / textconv (selected by .gitattributes or .git/info/attributes). Raw claude has no allowlist, so no new exposure there; the allowlist narrowed the tools but left a repo-controlled exec path inside Loki's own trust gate.
- Law broken: the repo under review is untrusted input (safe_git.ts FC-25); the evidence a reviewer reads must not be producible or mutable by the code under review.
- Cause (measured): safe_git hardens only Loki's own git calls (argv `-c` flags, blanked driver keys). A reviewer model builds its own git argv, and REVIEW_ALLOWLIST_TOKEN grants `Bash(git ...:*)` with no flag restriction, so nothing hardened the child's git. The only lever that reaches a model-built command is the child's env.
- Siblings swept (every spawn of claude or another provider CLI that can run git):
  - council voters and devil's advocate, loki-ts/src/council/voter_agents.ts defaultClaudeRunner: the only TS consumer of reviewAllowlistArgv. Fixed.
  - quality_gates code review, loki-ts/src/runner/quality_gates.ts (structured and text claude spawns): denylist only, no allowlist, but Bash is otherwise open so git status/diff is reachable. Fixed with the same env.
  - bash reviewer dispatch, autonomy/run.sh _dispatch_reviewer claude arm (the other consumer of loki_review_allowlist, three spawn sites): fixed through `loki internal reviewer-git-env`, fail-closed (no env, no allowlisted review).
  - run.sh codex, cline, aider and opencode reviewer arms: no shared git allowlist (that token is a claude CLI flag); their reviewers get a diff in the prompt. Not changed, recorded here as swept.
  - engine10 stages xreview.ts and deep.ts council session (providers.ts sessions): different mechanism (read-only disallowedTools on a session that already has exec); owned by the FC-XREVIEW-BLIND-TOOLS row and another engineer, not touched.
  - autonomy/grill.sh and engine10 worker sessions: no review allowlist; worker sessions already have full exec by design.
- Mechanism: one helper, `reviewerGitEnv` in loki-ts/src/util/safe_git.ts, reusing driverKeys (the DRIVER_KEY_RE enumeration). It sets GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM and command-scope GIT_CONFIG_COUNT entries (core.fsmonitor blank, core.hooksPath and core.attributesFile /dev/null, core.pager cat, protocol.ext.allow never, every repo filter and textconv key blanked, every diff.<driver>.command pointed at a plain `diff -u` shim) and replaces GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF with the same shim. Route chosen over dropping git from the allowlist: council prompts and reviewers rely on git diff and git show to read the change, and removing them would blind the reviewers (the FC-XREVIEW-BLIND-TOOLS class); env hardening keeps the capability and closes the exec path. A blank diff.external or driver command makes git die ("external diff died"), hence the shim instead of a blank.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/util/safe_git_extdiff.test.ts describe "FC-REVIEWER-GIT-CONFIG-EXEC reviewerGitEnv" (control: plain git fires fsmonitor, diff.external and the driver command; with the real builder none fire and status, diff, show -p and log -p still return real patches; wiring check on both TS spawns). Mutation-checked: dropping the driver-key enumeration, the fsmonitor entry or the GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF override each turns the fixture red.
- Round 2 (HIGH review block): the same mechanism now also covers gpg exec (log.showSignature=false and gpg.program, gpg.openpgp.program, gpg.ssh.program, gpg.x509.program set to false), partial-clone lazy fetch firing core.sshCommand (GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1 plus repo network command keys enumerated with NET_CMD_KEY_RE and blanked), a shim that ends options with `--` so a file named `-n` is a path, textconv blanked to `cat` (a blank one dies on log -p), and GIT_PAGER=cat. reviewerGitEnv takes a required base, and the two TS reviewer spawns pass tokenFreeEnv(process.env); full_env_spawn_guard.test.ts flags a reviewerGitEnv call with no base or a non-token-free base in the spawning reviewers. Fixtures in safe_git_extdiff.test.ts (gpg, lazy fetch, `-n`, textconv) and the guard test; each mutation (drop gpg entries, drop GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH, drop `--`, textconv to blank, non-token-free base) turns its fixture red.
- Round 3: remote.<n>.uploadpack/receivepack are first-value-wins, so a repo-local value beats an env blank. reviewerGitEnv therefore throws when the repo sets either key, and throws on a partial clone (extensions.partialClone) when git is older than 2.44 (GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH is ignored there); both fixtures are mutation-checked. The spawn guard now requires tokenFreeEnv( on every reviewerGitEnv call (per call, not per file); the only raw process.env base is the cli.ts reviewer-git-env print, an explicit file-and-line row that fails the guard when stale.

## FC-TRUSTED-PUSH-PARTIAL-CLONE The trusted push fetches the run branch out of a partial (--filter=blob:none) agent clone into a temp repo, and upload-pack dies serving objects the clone never downloaded, so a VERIFIED run ends with no PR
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. A VERIFIED loki 11.17.100 run on anthropics/claude-code#81826 (clone made with --filter=blob:none) ended `engine10-push: push to fork asklokesh/claude-code refused or failed (rc=1)` and opened no PR.
- Law broken: a verified result must reach the user on every supported checkout shape (full, shallow, partial); a push helper must not depend on the agent repo being able to serve every reachable object.
- Cause (measured): `_loki_trusted_push` fetches from the agent repo path into a fresh template-less repo. On a partial clone upload-pack runs pack-objects over history whose blobs were never downloaded and modern git sets no lazy fetch there: `git-pack-objects died with error ... possible repository corruption on the remote side`.
- Siblings swept: autonomy/lib/engine10-push.sh `_e10_local_push` (same fetch-from-path into a temp repo, left unchanged on purpose: its pinned origin is a local path, so a lazy blob fetch would read the operator's own local repo, which holds every object, and there is no remote network credential to seed; the partial-clone failure is a remote-origin shape); autonomy/lib/engine10-push-gitlab.sh (same fetch from "$dir"; fixed with the same seeding, kept logically equivalent in the child because it is a standalone script that cannot source run.sh, and tests/test-engine10-push-gitlab.sh runs it that way); loki-ts/src/runner/attempts.ts only calls the shell push and holds no copy. No other fetch or clone from the agent repo path exists in autonomy/ or loki-ts/src.
- Mechanism: in `_loki_trusted_push`, when the agent repo is partial (extensions.partialclone non-empty, or any remote.<n>.promisor true/yes/on/1, both read as data), the temp repo is seeded with the validated origin URL (captured before a fork destination overwrites it) as promisor remote `loki-base`, and its refs are fetched with --filter=blob:none under the credential runner. The agent pack is thin (deltas against blobs the temp repo knows only by id), so the client lazy-fetches those delta bases from loki-base during the agent fetch; that fetch therefore also runs under the runner, with the agent-dir upload-pack (the only process that reads agent config) started through `--upload-pack '/usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1 git upload-pack'`, so no token, helper config, HOME or lazy fetch reaches it. Option chosen over prefetching delta-base blobs (git may pick any blob in the edge trees as a base, so an enumeration is a heuristic that fails closed only on private origins and costs a tree walk on large repos) and over an unfiltered seed (downloads full history). GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 on both fetches. Non-partial repos take the old path with no extra network or runner. A failed seeding or agent fetch fails closed.
- Fixture: tests/test-engine10-push-gitlab.sh (partial-clone block, red before with "could not stage branch", green after) and tests/test-trusted-push-agent-config.sh "FC-TRUSTED-PUSH-PARTIAL-CLONE" block (a real blob:none clone of a local origin with a history-only blob; pushes the agent branch; asserts the non-partial path never writes remote.loki-base config). Red before (rc=1, no push), green after. Review round 1 (HIGH, reproduced): the agent fetch ran token-less, so a private origin refused the lazy delta-base fetch; both fixtures now add a 20000-line file edited by the agent, an origin that refuses token-less reads through a packObjectsHook in the test global config, and a spy on the agent-dir upload-pack environment. Red on 95bf13b9a for the GitHub path (rc=1, nothing pushed); the GitLab child holds its token process-wide, so only its env-scrub assertion was red there.

## FC-FULL-SUITE-EXIT-CODED-SCRIPT A project-model full-suite command that is a plain CI checker script (not a test framework) prints no runner summary, so verify recorded it not_run UNMEASURED and a proven change sealed PARTIAL
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. claude-code#81826 on v11.17.101 sealed PARTIAL although the Wall passed with n=7; Not-proven listed "one reviewer", "not run: full suite: repo-root" and "executed count unmeasured".
- Law broken: a run seals VERIFIED unless it genuinely cannot be proven; a harness-side parse gap on a command that has no summary to parse must not hold back a proven change.
- Cause (measured): the model gave package repo-root the test command `python3 .github/scripts/check_workflow_hardening.py`; it printed "OK: checked 3 job(s) ..." and exited 0; classifyCheck (kind test) needs a parsed count, ran() returned null, result not_run. crossReview returns null for any verdict other than VERIFIED or ALREADY_SATISFIED, so "one reviewer" stayed uncredited only because the run was already PARTIAL.
- Siblings swept: runFullSuite legacy per-runner path (commands come from FULL_SUITE_CMD, always frameworks, unchanged); base_guard and wall use real runners (unchanged).
- Mechanism: check_result.ts isExitCodedScript (every segment a plain interpreter-plus-script run, nothing naming a test framework or containing "test"; anything ambiguous is a framework) feeds classifyCheck exitCoded: pass on rc 0, fail on rc != 0, count "n/a (exit-coded check, not a test runner)", never an n, so it is never executed proof. A framework with no summary stays UNMEASURED. FC-16 on the target is unchanged.
- Fixture: tests/util/check_result.test.ts and tests/engine10/monorepo_cwd.test.ts (red before: missing export), tests/engine10/seal.test.ts (Wall n>0 plus exit-coded full suite is VERIFIED with one reviewer credited by a real review).

## FC-UNOWNED-FILE-OWNED-BY-TESTLESS-PACKAGE A changed file no package owns made every unrelated test-less package own the diff, so a proven run sealed PARTIAL
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. claude-code#81826 on 11.17.102 sealed PARTIAL with "full suite: package scripts defines no test command" although the diff touched only plugins/ralph-wiggum/{hooks/stop-hook.sh,scripts/setup-ralph-loop.sh} and the passing Wall (n=4) executed both scripts. Run e10-20261019T191337Z-08db.
- Law: ownership is positive evidence, never absence of an owner; an unowned file is proven or named, never silently passed or silently blamed on an unrelated package.
- Cause (measured): runPackageSuites `owns` returned true for any test-less package whenever any product file had no owning package.
- Siblings swept: execProves (reused as the proof), the proofFiles credit, quoted and unresolved paths (quoted still owned by all, fail-safe), FC-WALL-FILE-OWNS-EVERY-PACKAGE.
- Mechanism: a test-less package owns an unowned file only if it is the repo-root package. Unowned product files are proven by execProves (Wall red on base, green on head, whole product diff unowned); otherwise one NOT PROVEN note "changed file <f> belongs to no package and no passing check exercised it".
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/monorepo_cwd.test.ts tests FC-UNOWNED-FILE-OWNED-BY-TESTLESS-PACKAGE. Red on 00bc53d72 (5 fail), green after.

## FC-UNIT-BASE-IS-NOT-THE-JOURNAL-BASE A group unit started at origin/HEAD while the slice journal and resume key named local HEAD, so a receipt could be adopted for a base it never ran on
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. A fresh group run skipped a unit as "reused from the slice journal" although the unit's work was built on a different commit than the group's base; or a valid receipt was refused. Raw: backlog.py:199 gave a non-fix unit base "", worktree_prep.py _start_point then ran git fetch and started it at origin/HEAD, while run_group keyed the journal on baseRef (local HEAD).
- Law: a resume or adoption key names the base the work actually ran on; one base per unit, derived once.
- Cause (measured): only fix units carried an explicit base (fix_units.ts); non-fix units inherited an implicit one.
- Siblings swept: fix_units.ts (already explicit, group tip), backlog.py ref-to-SHA resolution (reused), worktree_prep._start_point (explicit base returned untouched, no fetch), integrate (starts the group branch at baseRef), engine10 intake baseSha (single run, rev-parse HEAD), autonomy/loki origin/HEAD readers (not unit bases).
- Mechanism: run_group sets base = baseRef on every non-fix unit before writing dag.json, so dispatch and the journal key use the same value. The adoption is further bound to the signed task sha256, the unit branch tip and the verified receipt. No time bound is needed: a key that names the base and card never matches work built on another base, and a moved base misses.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/seal_group.test.ts "#317: local HEAD ahead of origin/HEAD, every unit starts at the journal-key base". Red before the fix (unit base undefined), green after.

## FC-TRUSTED-CACHE-VERDICT fast_verify trusts an agent-writable content-hash cache as a fresh scan, so a planted entry (an empty list suppressing a high finding, or injected finding text) decides the verdict
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. A planted `.loki/cache/fast-verify.json` entry keyed by a file's content hash is returned as that file's findings without rescanning. `[]` makes a file with a high finding PASS; a crafted message reaches the MCP client as trusted tool output. Closed by #319.
- Law: a verdict is derived from the content being judged, never from a file the judged agent can write (EL exogenous gates).
- Cause (measured): autonomy/lib/fast_verify.py Cache reads entries by blake2b(content) and returns them unvalidated; mcp/server.py listed loki_verify_fast in _TRUSTED_TOOLS so its finding text skipped the untrusted envelope.
- Siblings swept (grep for cache files under .loki and ~/.loki in loki-ts/src, autonomy, mcp): engine10/cache.ts repomap/testmap cache lives under ~/.loki/cache/v10, outside the repo, keyed by tree hash, no MAC (same-user write is out of this row's threat model; it selects tests, it does not return findings); run.sh `.loki/cache/install` is in-repo and agent-writable but stores node_modules, not a verdict; router history and update_check feed estimates and notices, not a verdict. No other in-repo cache returns findings. Every MCP tool and resource is covered by the runtime-derived guard in tests/mcp/test_phase1_tools.py.
- Mechanism: two layers. (1) loki_verify_fast findings sit under untrusted_content via _UNTRUSTED_FIELDS. (2) fast_verify.Cache lives at ~/.loki/cache/fast-verify/<realpath hash>.json (LOKI_FAST_VERIFY_CACHE_DIR overrides), and each entry carries an HMAC under a per-user 0600 key beside it. The cache key and the MAC body are derived from everything the verdict depends on: a producer fingerprint (blake2b of fast_verify.py's own bytes, computed at import, so any rule edit orphans every old entry and there is no hand-bumped schema constant), the repo-relative path (is_test decides which rules run, so a file moved from a test path to a product path is rescanned), and the content. An entry whose MAC fails is a miss and the file is rescanned; with no readable key, no readable source or an unusable cache dir the cache is off. The key is created through a 0600 temp file and os.link (never clobbered, never partial), the cache dir is forced to 0700 even when it pre-exists loose, a key that is group or other accessible or not owned by the user is refused, and the cache json is written at 0600 through a temp file and os.replace. The old repo-local `.loki/cache/fast-verify.json` is never read. Closes #319.
- Trust boundary: closed for repo content and for any write the judged agent makes inside the repo. NOT closed against a same-user process that can read ~/.loki/cache/fast-verify/key: it can forge a valid MAC. A same-user agent that is allowed to read the home directory therefore defeats this cache; the only complete answer for that threat is --no-cache (the fast scan is milliseconds), which the exogenous gates should use.
- Sibling not closed (engine10 cache): loki-ts/src/engine10/cache.ts keys repomap and testmap by HEAD^{tree} plus a hand-bumped "repomap2-" prefix, so a builder change does not invalidate stored maps (same class as the hand-bumped CACHE_SCHEMA removed here). It selects tests and does not return findings, so it is not a verdict, but the fix is the same idea: derive the builder version from the builder's bytes at build time instead of a manual prefix. Tracked as a follow-up, not changed here because the TS bundle has no stable source path to hash at runtime.
- Fixture: tests/mcp/test_phase1_tools.py test_stored_text_appears_only_inside_untrusted_content (red with loki_verify_fast trusted: leaks ['loki_verify_fast']; green after) and tests/test-fast-verify.sh CACHE BINDING block (planted repo-local and forged-MAC empty-list entries; red when the MAC check is replaced by `if False:`, green with it), plus the CACHE KEY and CACHE FILES blocks there: a test-path file moved to a product path must FAIL (red when the path is dropped from cache_key), a copy of fast_verify.py with disabled rules must not poison the real rules' cache (red when PRODUCER is a fixed string), the key and cache json are 0600 and the dir 0700 (red when the key is written 0666), and no verify cache file appears in a repo (red when cache_path points at root/.loki/cache/fast-verify.json). Tests that run fast_verify set LOKI_FAST_VERIFY_CACHE_DIR to a run tmp or pass --no-cache, never the real ~/.loki.

## FC-CATALOG-CAPABILITY-DRIFT-UNCHECKED A catalog entry whose capabilities drifted from the API failed only at request time
- User-visible: a catalog entry whose thinking types or effort levels drifted from what the model accepts fails only at request time, as an API error mid-run. Raw: the catalog (providers/model_catalog.json) is static and never compared with models.retrieve.
- Law broken: a capability the harness relies on is claimed without being checked against its source of truth.
- Siblings swept: effortForTier clamp (bash and Bun, parity-checked), claudeEffortLevels, the catalog freshness row (age only, offline).
- Mechanism: loki doctor "Model capabilities" row. With ANTHROPIC_API_KEY it calls models.retrieve per claude catalog id and prints each mismatch (effort levels, API-expressible thinking types); without a key or offline it warns and never changes the tally or exit code. The bench runner takes --effort sweep, expanding to the catalog levels for --model.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/commands/doctor.test.ts "model capability row", tests/test_bench_runner.py sweep tests, tests/test-bash-bun-parity.sh effort clamp cells. Red when the comparison, the sweep catalog read or the clamp model argument is removed.

- Mechanism: run_group sets base = baseRef on every non-fix unit before writing dag.json, so dispatch and the journal key use the same value. The adoption is further bound to the signed task sha256, the unit branch tip and the verified receipt. #317b: task.sha256 covers items only, so the receipt also signs unit_spec.sha256 (unitSpecSha256 in unit_mode.ts over id, items, acceptance, writeSet, base), written by the seal stage and recomputed by adoptJournaled from the same function; an absent, empty or mismatched value rejects the adoption. No time bound is needed: a key that names the base and card never matches work built on another base, and a moved base misses.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/seal_group.test.ts "#317: local HEAD ahead of origin/HEAD, every unit starts at the journal-key base". Red before the fix (unit base undefined), green after. #317b fixtures: "a colliding key whose signed unit spec has a different acceptance is not adopted" and "a receipt with no signed unit_spec is not adopted", red with the spec check removed or acceptance left out of the hash.

## FC-TEST-TIMEOUT-OVERRIDES-CLI A file-level setDefaultTimeout(30_000) overrides the bun test --timeout flag and ignores machine load, so a slow-but-correct test fails on a busy host
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible to every engineer and release gate. `runAutonomous > repo profile cache hit` failed at 30.1s on origin/main at load average 28 on 14 cores, both with `--timeout 30000` and with `--timeout 120000`. The same test passed with the literal raised to 240_000 (87s wall, including startup).
- Law: a test's time budget scales with the load of the machine it runs on, and the operator has an env knob that always works; a flake is a missing scale, not a reason to retry. bun test does not expose `--timeout` to the process (argv, Bun.argv and execArgv were probed under `bun test --timeout 777777`: argv=[bun, file]), so the knob is an env var, not the CLI flag.
- Cause (measured): 14 test files call `setDefaultTimeout(N)` with a fixed literal (10 at 30_000, 3 at 130_000, 1 at 60_000; count: `git grep -n 'setLoadAwareTimeout(' -- loki-ts/tests` after the change, `git grep -n 'setDefaultTimeout(' fff00b1da^ -- loki-ts/tests` before), and a file-level call wins over the CLI flag.
- Siblings swept: all 14 literals in loki-ts/tests, replaced in one change; per-test `, N)` timeouts are explicit single-test budgets and are not in this class.
- Mechanism: loki-ts/tests/preload.ts exports `setLoadAwareTimeout(baseMs)`: baseMs times clamp(ceil(2 * loadavg1 / cpus), 1, 10). `LOKI_TEST_TIMEOUT_SCALE` (positive number, capped at 10) replaces the factor; `LOKI_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` is a floor that wins when larger. A guard in loki-ts/tests/util fails on any direct `setDefaultTimeout(` call in tests.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/util/test_timeout_guard.test.ts tests FC-TEST-TIMEOUT-OVERRIDES-CLI, including a subprocess run through the real setLoadAwareTimeout path (env knob lifts the budget, a real hang still times out).

- Siblings swept: every registered MCP tool and resource, via the runtime-derived guard in tests/mcp/test_phase1_tools.py (seeds this cache with the marker; the observer now also sees os.scandir, os.stat, Path.iterdir and glob); the JS mirror declares its fields in src/protocols and counts them in tests/protocols/mcp-server.test.js.
- Mechanism: part 1 (this change) moves loki_verify_fast findings under untrusted_content via _UNTRUSTED_FIELDS. The verdict-suppression half is open in #319 (cache entries must be authenticated or re-derived, then the row is closed).
- Fixture: tests/mcp/test_phase1_tools.py test_stored_text_appears_only_inside_untrusted_content (red with loki_verify_fast trusted: leaks ['loki_verify_fast']; green after).

## FC-STATE-MANAGER-ROOT-IGNORED A process-wide state manager singleton ignored its lokiDir after first use, and a per-root cache would grow without bound
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. getStateManager({ lokiDir }) for a second .loki directory returned the first directory's manager, so reads and writes for run B landed in run A's state; a long-lived process serving many roots would have leaked one open watcher per root once keyed naively.
- Law: a keyed accessor honours its key on every call; a cache that holds OS resources is bounded and releases what it evicts.
- Cause (measured): state/manager.ts kept one module-level defaultManager and created it from the first call's options only.
- Siblings swept: state/manager.py get_state_manager has the same single-default shape and is to follow the same keying rule (separate branch); no other TS caller passes arguments to getStateManager.
- Mechanism: one per-root cache in state/manager.ts keyed by the (st_dev, st_ino) of the .loki directory (symlinks and case spellings share a manager), capped by LOKI_STATE_MANAGER_MAX (default 8), least recently used evicted and stop()ped.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/state_manager.test.ts (under loki-ts so bun and CI discover it). Red on origin/main (2 fail), green after (5 pass or 4 pass and 1 skip on a case-sensitive volume); mutations: no eviction, eviction without stop, path-string key, constant key, no LRU touch each go red.

- Fixture: tests/mcp/test_phase1_tools.py test_stored_text_appears_only_inside_untrusted_content (red with loki_verify_fast trusted: leaks ['loki_verify_fast']; green after).


## FC-RELEASE-GREEN-WITHOUT-REGISTRY-PROOF A green publish-npm job proved only that npm accepted the upload, not that the registry served the version
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. Run 39852427194 printed "+ loki-mode@11.50.0" at 20:52:55Z and "+latest" at 20:52:57Z, but the registry did not serve 11.50.0 until about 21:04Z. A consumer installing in that window got the old version from a release reported green.
- Law: a release is green on registry proof, not on a client's upload log; the proof is a read of the registry.
- Cause (measured): the registry applies a publish asynchronously. The registry's own time field for 11.53.0, 11.53.1 and 11.54.0 is 4m10s, 5m13s and 6m12s after the workflow's "+ loki-mode@" line.
- Siblings swept: publish-ts-sdk and publish-python-sdk (same upload-log-only pattern; not changed here, listed as follow-up), npm-dist-tags.sh (re-reads tags after writing).
- Mechanism: job verify-npm-registry (needs publish-npm only, no concurrency group, nothing needs it) polls `npm view loki-mode@VERSION version --prefer-online` for about 15 minutes and fails red if it never resolves. It cannot delay docker, homebrew, tag or release, nor hold the release-npm lock.
- Fixture: tests/test_release_next_tag.py test_registry_proof_is_its_own_job_and_nothing_waits_on_it. Red when the job is renamed or removed (mutant run), green after.

## FC-RELEASE-NOTES-FORMAT-UNCHECKED-LOCALLY release.sh --ship wrote a CHANGELOG section CI's release-notes.sh rejects, so the failure surfaced only after npm had published and the tag and GitHub release were skipped
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. v11.53.0, v11.53.1 and v11.54.0 were on npm with no tag and no GitHub release: "section ... is not fully written (no '### ' subsection)".
- Law: a local release gate runs the same validators CI runs before the commit and push; no validator that can block a downstream channel runs only after an irreversible publish.
- Cause (measured): ship_write_changelog copied the --notes file verbatim and only compared the header line; scripts/release-notes.sh ran first in CI's release job.
- Siblings swept: the global guard rows (scripts/global-guards.tsv) already run locally; the pre-push hook's release-notes gate checks the tree, not the --notes file before the write.
- Mechanism: run_ship calls ship_check_notes_format (scripts/release-notes.sh on the notes file) in preflight, before any write, and again on the written CHANGELOG.md; both refuse with SHIP_NOTES_FORMAT (exit 4). LOKI_RELEASE_SKIP_GUARDS does not reach it.
- Fixture: tests/test-release-sh.sh T17d (flat notes with LOKI_RELEASE_SKIP_GUARDS=1). Red on origin/main's release.sh (exit 0, VERSION bumped, pushed), green after.

## FC-DIST-TAG-MOVED-BACKWARDS Concurrent back-to-back releases left npm latest below the newest version
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. After 11.53.0, 11.53.1 and 11.54.0 shipped within 8 minutes, dist-tags read latest=11.53.1 and next=11.54.0, so `npm i loki-mode` installed an older release than the newest.
- Law: a dist-tag only ever moves to the highest published version, and the final state is read back, not assumed from the write.
- Cause (measured, partly inferred): publish-npm logs show the writes in order (11.53.1 "+latest" 23:31:01Z, 11.54.0 "+latest" 23:34:42Z), so the runs did not run out of order and the lagging CUR read could only cause a skip, not a backwards move. The registry's time field shows 11.53.1 became served at 23:36:13Z, after 11.54.0's tag write; the observed latest=11.53.1 is consistent with the registry applying 11.53.1's tag write late. That mechanism is inferred from timestamps, not directly observed.
- Siblings swept: publish-ts-sdk had the same CUR-vs-VERSION compare for loki-mode-sdk; fixed in slice pd/sdktags, it now calls scripts/npm-dist-tags.sh with LOKI_DISTTAG_PKG=loki-mode-sdk (no second copy of the logic). publish-python-sdk has no dist-tag step and needs none: PyPI has no tags and orders releases by version, so a late upload of a lower version cannot become the latest the installer resolves (twine --skip-existing keeps reruns idempotent). The CoS-dispatched force_republish run 39898474372 re-asserted 11.54.0 at 04:06:43Z.
- Mechanism: scripts/npm-dist-tags.sh targets max(VERSION, latest, next, every published stable version), never lower; it re-reads both tags after writing and again after a pause, re-asserting the target until both settle or the tries run out (red). Every read (latest, next, published versions) must succeed and be non-empty before any write; an error or empty answer refuses with "refusing to move latest (fail closed)" and zero writes (review rev-a: an outage otherwise made VERSION the only candidate).
- Fixture: tests/test-npm-dist-tags.sh (7 cases incl. late overtaking write restored and view outage error/empty with zero writes). Mutants (ignore tags, no re-read, target=VERSION, outage guard removed) each fail a case.

- Mechanism: scripts/npm-dist-tags.sh targets max(VERSION, latest, next, every published stable version), never lower than its own reads; a per-tag read above the target (another run moved it) or an empty one re-reads and writes nothing, and npm has no compare-and-set so a window of milliseconds between the last read and the write remains; it re-reads both tags after writing and again after a pause, re-asserting the target until both settle or the tries run out (red). Every read (latest, next, published versions) must succeed and be non-empty before any write; an error or empty answer refuses with "refusing to move latest (fail closed)" and zero writes (review rev-a: an outage otherwise made VERSION the only candidate).
- Fixture: tests/test-npm-dist-tags.sh (8 cases incl. a fresh-higher read never written backwards; incl. late overtaking write restored and view outage error/empty with zero writes). Mutants (ignore tags, no re-read, target=VERSION, outage guard removed) each fail a case.

- Late materialization (recurrence 2026-10-23): the registry applies a publish 3 to 6 minutes after the client prints "+ loki-mode@X" (registry time field: 11.53.0 +4m10s, 11.53.1 +5m13s, 11.54.0 +6m12s). 11.54.1 uploaded 04:34:24Z, served 04:39:34Z; 11.54.2 uploaded 04:38:07Z, served 04:41:17Z, leaving latest=11.54.1 next=11.54.2. The 15s settle in the script is far shorter than the lag, so a write made in publish-npm can be overtaken after it settles (mechanism inferred, not directly observed). Fix: verify-npm-registry (and verify-sdk-registry for loki-mode-sdk) run scripts/npm-dist-tags.sh again after the version resolves; the script is convergent (max stable, never lower), so it is safe outside the release-npm lock.
- Fixture: tests/test-npm-dist-tags.sh (9 cases incl. latest dragged down after the first settle restored by a second run, a settled converge run writing nothing, late overtaking write restored and view outage error/empty with zero writes). Mutants (ignore tags, no re-read, target=VERSION, outage guard removed) each fail a case.

## FC-CATALOG-CAPABILITY-DRIFT-UNCHECKED A catalog entry whose capabilities drifted from the API failed only at request time
- User-visible: a catalog entry whose thinking types or effort levels drifted from what the model accepts fails only at request time, as an API error mid-run. Raw: the catalog (providers/model_catalog.json) is static and never compared with models.retrieve.
- Law broken: a capability the harness relies on is claimed without being checked against its source of truth.
- Siblings swept: effortForTier clamp (bash and Bun, parity-checked), claudeEffortLevels, the catalog freshness row (age only, offline).
- Mechanism: loki doctor "Model capabilities" row. With ANTHROPIC_API_KEY it calls models.retrieve per claude catalog id and prints each mismatch (effort levels, API-expressible thinking types); without a key or offline it warns and never changes the tally or exit code. The bench runner takes --effort sweep, expanding to the catalog levels for --model.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/commands/doctor.test.ts "model capability row", tests/test_bench_runner.py sweep tests, tests/test-bash-bun-parity.sh effort clamp cells. Red when the comparison, the sweep catalog read or the clamp model argument is removed.
