- Mechanism: run_group sets base = baseRef on every non-fix unit before writing dag.json, so dispatch and the journal key use the same value. The adoption is further bound to the signed task sha256, the unit branch tip and the verified receipt. #317b: task.sha256 covers items only, so the receipt also signs unit_spec.sha256 (unitSpecSha256 in unit_mode.ts over id, items, acceptance, writeSet, base), written by the seal stage and recomputed by adoptJournaled from the same function; an absent, empty or mismatched value rejects the adoption. No time bound is needed: a key that names the base and card never matches work built on another base, and a moved base misses.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/seal_group.test.ts "#317: local HEAD ahead of origin/HEAD, every unit starts at the journal-key base". Red before the fix (unit base undefined), green after. #317b fixtures: "a colliding key whose signed unit spec has a different acceptance is not adopted" and "a receipt with no signed unit_spec is not adopted", red with the spec check removed or acceptance left out of the hash.

## FC-TEST-TIMEOUT-OVERRIDES-CLI A file-level setDefaultTimeout(30_000) overrides the bun test --timeout flag and ignores machine load, so a slow-but-correct test fails on a busy host
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible to every engineer and release gate. `runAutonomous > repo profile cache hit` failed at 30.1s on origin/main at load average 28 on 14 cores, both with `--timeout 30000` and with `--timeout 120000`. The same test passed with the literal raised to 240_000 (87s wall, including startup).
- Law: a test's time budget scales with the load of the machine it runs on, and the operator has an env knob that always works; a flake is a missing scale, not a reason to retry. bun test does not expose `--timeout` to the process (argv, Bun.argv and execArgv were probed under `bun test --timeout 777777`: argv=[bun, file]), so the knob is an env var, not the CLI flag.
- Cause (measured): 14 test files call `setDefaultTimeout(N)` with a fixed literal (10 at 30_000, 3 at 130_000, 1 at 60_000; count: `git grep -n 'setLoadAwareTimeout(' -- loki-ts/tests` after the change, `git grep -n 'setDefaultTimeout(' fff00b1da^ -- loki-ts/tests` before), and a file-level call wins over the CLI flag.
- Siblings swept: all 14 literals in loki-ts/tests, replaced in one change; per-test `, N)` timeouts are explicit single-test budgets and are not in this class.
- Mechanism: loki-ts/tests/preload.ts exports `setLoadAwareTimeout(baseMs)`: baseMs times clamp(ceil(2 * loadavg1 / cpus), 1, 10). `LOKI_TEST_TIMEOUT_SCALE` (positive number, capped at 10) replaces the factor; `LOKI_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` is a floor that wins when larger. A guard in loki-ts/tests/util fails on any direct `setDefaultTimeout(` call in tests.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/util/test_timeout_guard.test.ts tests FC-TEST-TIMEOUT-OVERRIDES-CLI, including a subprocess run through the real setLoadAwareTimeout path (env knob lifts the budget, a real hang still times out).

- Late materialization (recurrence 2026-10-23): the registry applies a publish 3 to 6 minutes after the client prints "+ loki-mode@X" (registry time field: 11.53.0 +4m10s, 11.53.1 +5m13s, 11.54.0 +6m12s). 11.54.1 uploaded 04:34:24Z, served 04:39:34Z; 11.54.2 uploaded 04:38:07Z, served 04:41:17Z, leaving latest=11.54.1 next=11.54.2. The 15s settle in the script is far shorter than the lag, so a write made in publish-npm can be overtaken after it settles (mechanism inferred, not directly observed). Fix: verify-npm-registry runs scripts/npm-dist-tags.sh again after loki-mode@VERSION resolves; the script is convergent (max stable, never lower), so it is safe outside the release-npm lock.
- Fixture: tests/test-npm-dist-tags.sh (9 cases incl. latest dragged down after the first settle restored by a second run, a settled converge run writing nothing, late overtaking write restored and view outage error/empty with zero writes). Mutants (ignore tags, no re-read, target=VERSION, outage guard removed) each fail a case.

## FC-THINKING-TYPE-ACCEPTED-BY-CLI-REJECTED-BY-API A thinking type the CLI accepted was a 400 for the model, and the usage error stated a rule the model did not have
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki internal sdk-judge|sdk-text --model claude-sonnet-5-5 --thinking disabled` at effort high or below was sent as `thinking: {type: "disabled"}`, which the API rejects with a 400 at every effort for that model (claude-api skill, Thinking & Effort table: Sonnet 5.5 "disabled returns 400; to turn thinking off send between_tools"). The judge failed closed and the bash caller fell back. Separately, the usage error "disabled cannot combine with --effort xhigh or max" was printed for claude-opus-5 and claude-haiku-5-5, which #314 clamps instead of refusing.
- Law: a request parameter is derived from the model catalog, never hand-written per model; a message never states a rule the catalog does not record.
- Cause (measured): #314 encoded only the effort ceiling (thinking.max_effort) and kept one model-blind sentence in two callers; the catalog had no way to say that a type is never valid for a model and what replaces it.
- Siblings swept: claude-opus-5-5, claude-fable-5-1 and claude-fable-5 list only adaptive in thinking.types, so disabled is a 400 at every effort for them (review of 37d4eb08d): claudeThinkingAccepted(model, type) is false for a type absent from types with no substitute, thinkingConflictsEffort refuses it at every effort, thinkingUsageError says "disabled is not accepted by <model>", and clampThinkingForEffort sends adaptive with a ledger record; claude-haiku-4-5 (no disabled substitute is invented), the max_effort clamp (still applied to the substituted type), thinkingConflictsEffort (a substituted model no longer conflicts), both callers of the usage error.
- Mechanism: catalog thinking.substitute maps a requested type to the type sent (claude-sonnet-5-5: disabled to between_tools). clampThinkingForEffort applies the substitute, then the max_effort clamp to the substituted type, and the ledger thinking_clamp records `disabled->between_tools@<effort> (max high)` or `disabled->adaptive@<effort> (max high)`. thinkingUsageError builds the sentence from the substitute and max_effort of the resolved model.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/providers/claude_flags.test.ts "thinking substitute and usage message (#314 follow-up)", loki-ts/tests/runner/sdk_invoker.test.ts "thinking substitute (#314)", tests/test-model-catalog-single-source.sh pins thinking.substitute. Three mutants (drop the catalog substitute, hand-write the message, clamp on the requested type) each turn them red.

## FC-PRINTED-COMMAND-REACHES-WRONG-ENGINE The two commands the product prints for a user (`loki start --resume`, `loki verify <receipt path>`) were routed to an engine that could not do the job, and reported success or a wrong verdict
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. After a crash in implement, `loki start --resume` printed "phase implement is not auto-resumable ... start a new run", resumed nothing and exited 0 in 0.46s (the stage resume resumed the same crash in 20.8s through `loki "<task>" --resume`). `loki verify .loki/runs/<id>/receipt.json` on a VERIFIED run printed `VERDICT: CONCERNS` with the receipt path used as a git base ref ("Diff: 0 files"), where bare `loki verify` said VERIFIED.
- Law: a command the product tells the user to run reaches the engine that owns the artifact it names, and its exit code is the engine's, never a success the engine did not report.
- Cause (measured): the legacy start branch delegated `engine10 resume` (resumeMain, the reporter) instead of `engine10 --resume` (resumeFlag, the stage resume) and mapped its exit 4 to 0; cmd_verify routed only composite, group and deploy receipts to engine10, so a run receipt fell through to the diff verifier. tests/test-next-resume-agree.sh pinned `STUB-ARGV:engine10 resume` with a stub that exits 0, so it passed while the user path resumed nothing (a guard that passed its own mutation).
- Siblings swept: `loki resume` (the reporter, keeps its 0/4/5 map), `loki "<task>" --resume` (already resumeFlag), bare `loki verify` and `loki verify e10-*` (already v10 in bin/loki), `--pubkey` verify (already v10), a git ref and a non-receipt .json (stay on the diff verifier, pinned as controls).
- Mechanism: `start --resume` delegates `engine10 --resume [run-id]` and returns the code unchanged; cmd_verify sends `.loki/runs/e10-*/receipt.json` paths and any existing file whose schema starts `loki.v10.receipt` to `engine10 verify`.
- Fixture: tests/test-next-resume-agree.sh drives the real Bun entry on a seeded interrupted run (asserts `engine10: resume from stage implement`, exit 2 not 0) and a stub that exits 4 (asserts 4); tests/test-verify-receipt-route.sh covers the path forms, a tampered receipt (TAMPERED, never CONCERNS) and the diff-verifier controls. Red on origin/main (17 and 4 failures), green after; mutants (delegate back to `resume`, schema route removed) turn them red.



## FC-EMPTY-RUN-BRANCH-LEFT-CHECKED-OUT A run that committed nothing left HEAD on its empty loki/ branch, the next run stacked on it, and the handoff offered to merge a 0-file change
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. After two FAILED runs with 0 commits, two empty `loki/e10-*` branches remained, HEAD sat on the last one (the next run printed `base: loki/e10-...`), and HANDOFF.md read `Accept: git merge --no-ff loki/e10-...` for 0 files.
- Law: a run never leaves the user's checkout worse than it found it (Claude Code never moves your branch); only work that exists is offered for acceptance.
- Cause (measured): stages/intake.ts ensureBranch checks out the run branch; supervisor.ts restored origBranch only on a stop signal, and run_summary.ts always printed the Accept/Reject pair.
- Siblings swept: stop (SIGINT/SIGTERM) already restored via restoreBranch and keeps the branch (a stopped run is resumable); crashed runs never reach the terminal code, so resume keeps its branch. A run branch with any commit, a branch the user moved off, and a non-`loki/` name are never touched.
- Legacy sibling (run.sh): the session branch `loki/session-*` had the same defect. `_loki_restore_base_branch` (autonomy/run.sh) applies the same rule at session end: only for a non-zero result other than a stop signal (130, 143), only when the branch tip equals `session-start-sha`, never a branch with a commit, a dirty tracked tree keeps the branch and prints the way back, and the delete is `git branch -d`. A successful (VERIFIED) session and a stopped one are left alone, as in engine10. Fixture: tests/test-branch-lifecycle.sh section BR (empty failed session, failed with commit, successful, signal, dirty tree, and a mutant that drops the tip check). The engine10 CLI path was checked on main with an invalid-API-key stub run through fatal_e2e: HEAD returned to main and no loki/ branch remained, so no engine10 gap exists.
- Mechanism: stop_restore.ts dropEmptyRunBranch, called once from the supervisor after the backstop commit, deletes the branch (update-ref -d with the expected tip) only when the tip equals intake's base_sha, after restoreBranch's plain no-force checkout; run_summary.ts prints `Nothing to accept` for 0 files.
- Fixture: tests/engine10/supervisor_signal.test.ts "supervisor: a run that committed nothing" (empty, committed positive control, user-switched, dirty, non-loki name) and tests/util/run_summary.test.ts (0-file Decide block).

## FC-GROUP-REFUSES-LOCAL-TASK-WITHOUT-ORIGIN A multi-part prompt in a repo with no owner/repo origin exits NOT PROVEN with no unit started, so a task that needs no remote cannot run at all
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. `loki start` on a two-file prompt in a fresh local repo printed "NOT PROVEN: group run needs an origin remote of the form owner/repo; no unit was started" and exited 1 in 0.2 s. The bench (fd-2-two-module-untested, loki arm, 2026-10-23) scored it a failure although Loki never attempted the task.
- Law broken: a path that needs a remote only for its launcher must be chosen only when the remote exists; a local task never fails because of a capability it did not need (verified-unless-it-cannot-be).
- Cause (measured): route.ts chose the group runner on decomposability and provider slots alone; run_group.ts then refused at its own origin check (the unit launcher, autonomy/lib/backlog.py, takes the owner/repo slug).
- Siblings swept (grep for remote get-url origin across loki-ts/src, autonomy, bin): run_group.ts (three checks, now one originSlug); backlog.py origin_slug (the launcher itself, github.com only); run_outputs.ts resume (already degrades with a message); base_guard.ts (already skips without an origin); issue-parser.sh and run.sh issue-mode slug detection (issue mode needs a repo by definition). No other local-task path requires an origin.
- Mechanism: route.ts asks run_group.originSlug before choosing the real group runner (task or mission) and otherwise takes the existing sequential fallback with the reason named. run_group.ts keeps its own refusal for a direct call.
- Sibling not closed: originSlug accepts any path-shaped origin with two segments (a local bare path reads as a slug), while backlog.py accepts github.com only; tightening it would change GitHub Enterprise hosts, so it is left and noted here.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/engine10/route_group.test.ts (no origin: sequential with reason, not NOT PROVEN, task and mission; control: an owner/repo origin still reaches the group runner). Red with the two gates in route.ts removed (3 failures).

## FC-SKILL-LINK-HIJACK A plain run rewrites the user's global skill link to itself
- User saw: running any loki binary repointed ~/.claude/skills/loki-mode, once into a loki-run temp dir and later, on `loki --version` from global 11.17.103, back to that older install.
- Mechanism (measured): bin/loki calls `loki_skill_link_heal` on every run (FC-30). The heal repointed a LIVE link to a different Loki install whenever the running root looked like an install path, with no check on temp roots, on which install was newer, or on whether the caller was an install step. Raw cause is a repair that treated "any running install" as the owner of the link.
- Law: L0 (repair only what is provably the harness's own, never take over a user-visible global). L2 (the verdict about the host must not depend on which binary was run last).
- Siblings: swept every writer of provider skill links. `loki_skill_link_heal` (all four provider dirs, one mechanism, fixed here); `cmd_setup_skill` (autonomy/loki ~14954) is an explicit command and refuses an existing target, so it stays; `cmd_remote` (autonomy/loki ~29612) only creates when SKILL.md is absent, never replaces a valid link; doctor.ts is read-only; shell completions do not touch skill dirs.
- Mechanism of the fix: one shared function. A plain run (mode run) repairs only dangling links; only `bin/loki-postinstall.js` (mode postinstall) repoints a live link to another install. A running root under TMPDIR, /tmp, /var/tmp or /var/folders is refused. A live link is never repointed to a lower VERSION than its current target. Opt-out LOKI_NO_SKILL_LINK_HEAL=1 unchanged.
- Fixture: tests/test-skill-link-heal.sh cases 15-18 (temp-rooted scratch prefix refused, plain run leaves a valid link, downgrade refused, upgrade by postinstall repoints as positive control), fake HOME only. Red on the old lib (3 of 4 fail, the control passes). Mutants: drop temp check, drop mode gate, drop version guard each turn exactly one case red.

## FC-FALSE-DONE-HAS-NO-BASELINE The bench had no task on which either tool claims done while a hidden check fails, so the false-done rate (M5) could not be measured as a rate
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. The claim "Loki stops on agent-independent checks, Claude Code stops when the model says done" had no number behind it: the only discriminator, multifail-1-two-modules, went 2 of 2 for both tools.
- Law broken: a comparative claim ships with a measured baseline and an interval, never with a rate inferred from tasks that do not separate the tools.
- Siblings swept: attribution.py (false_done, false_completion_rate) and runner.py grade already compute the metric and are unchanged; the existing tasks all pass on both tools in the recorded trials, so none separates them.
- Mechanism: four pre-registered tasks (benchmarks/bench/tasks/fd-1 to fd-4), each with a held-out overlay that checks only prompt-stated requirements. References and near-miss artifacts stay outside the repo and are hash-attested through run.sh oracles (private_probe.py). The task and overlay bytes are pinned, so a change after a paid trial needs a new task id.
- Fixture: tests/test_bench_taskspec.py FalseDonePreRegisteredTasks (pins, task load, overlay red on the untouched fixture). Red when an overlay or task byte changes.

## FC-CITATION-UNDER-SUBMODULE-ALWAYS-HIGH A fabricated blocking finding citing a path under a bumped submodule stayed HIGH, checked out or not, and said nothing
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. A council reviewer citing `vendor/lib/ghost.ts:2` for a file that exists nowhere kept a blocking HIGH finding and blocked the run, with no hint the citation could not be checked. Raw: loki-ts/src/council/finding_schema.ts citationVerdict returned "ok" for any path under a diff-listed gitlink, or whose directory component equalled a gitlink's final segment (#332, follow-up to #302).
- Law: a downgrade needs positive evidence, and an unchecked citation must say it was unchecked; "ok" is never the silent answer to "could not look".
- Siblings swept: touched() prefix rule, the loop resolving a path under a gitlink against cwd and root, rule (i) bare basename, rule (ii) final-segment component; all four now share one gitlink check (checkedOut, deleted-in-diff).
- Mechanism: citationVerdict returns "refuted" | "unproven" | "ok". A gitlink directory with content on disk and no deleted-in-diff record (CitationContext.gitlinkDeleted, filled by voter_agents from the submodule old..new diff) refutes the citation (MEDIUM, [citation not found]); an absent or empty gitlink directory keeps HIGH and prefixes the description with [NOT PROVEN: ...], which travels in the council issue text.
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/council/finding_schema.test.ts "submodule citation: checked out", "not checked out" and "deleted in the diff"; red when the return in the gitlink branch of citationVerdict is mutated back to "ok".

## FC-PYTEST-IMPORT-SYMLINK-UNDER-TESTS A committed change carried a symlink under tests/ (tests/kvparse.py, tests/wcl.py in 2 of 3 cw20 pilots), flagged only after the fact as NOT PROVEN by base_guard.ts (FC-WALL-PHANTOM-PATH)
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible; the PR holds a link the task never asked for and the verdict degrades to NOT PROVEN.
- Law broken: the change contains only what the task needs.
- Cause (SUSPECTED, evidence status: code reading only, no transcript yet): no engine call creates a link under tests/ (engine links are node_modules, .venv, venv in throwaway worktrees: base_guard.ts, mutation_proof.ts, before_after.ts; treeswap.ts restores an attempt's own link). The implement agent most likely links `tests/<mod>.py` to a root module so a bare `pytest` import works; the commit stage's `git add -A` then stages it. The Wall brief already carried `python -m pytest <file>` (wall_hints.ts), the implement brief did not.
- Siblings swept: fix.ts and other stages build on FIXED_RULES or a brief without a runner line; the Wall brief already states the invocation. Only the implement lead lacked it.
- Mechanism: one line in FIXED_RULES (cache-stable lead of the implement brief) naming the exact invocation and forbidding files or links under tests/ to satisfy imports.
- Fixture: tests/e10ext/full_job_brief.test.ts "FC-PYTEST-IMPORT-SYMLINK-UNDER-TESTS" and the implement-brief golden.
- Open: confirm by grepping the loki implement transcripts of the fd-1 and fd-4 grid trials for `ln -s` and bare `pytest` (eng-303c); update this row with the result. Not covered: an agent that links anyway is still only flagged, not blocked.

## FC-SHARD-WATCHLIST-STALE The shard-coverage guard hardcoded which five suites are the heaviest; the table moved on, and one new registration shifted LPT tie rotation and turned main red
- User-visible vs raw: raw (CI guard). tests/test-shard-coverage.sh T5 failed on main: "expected 5 watched suites in 5 distinct shards", with trust-core tests and E2e Features both in shard 1. It is also a misleading signal, because no shard was unbalanced.
- Law: a guard asserts the property that holds by construction, over inputs derived from the live data, never over a name list that was true on one date.
- Cause (measured): bisected across the four commits that touched the runner or the table. 54700a30b: 0 failures; 71b292af4 (registers npm-dist-tags.sh with no duration row): 1 failure; 4b880492b and 77f1196c5: 1 failure. The hardcoded watch list held "E2e Features" (20s in the table) and "Review deadline, ..." (45s), which stopped being among the five heaviest long ago (ShellCheck 140, trust-core 130, reachability 95, Quick receipt order 90, v10 ops 70). They only separated by tie rotation, and the new registration moved the rotation. LPT guarantees the heaviest n suites land in n distinct shards; light suites carry no such guarantee.
- Siblings swept: test-shard-durations-drift.sh failed on the same commit for the same missing row (npm-dist-tags.sh); fixed with a measured row (8s). T1 to T4 do not name suites.
- Mechanism: T5 derives its five suites from tests/shard-durations.tsv, restricted to registered names, sorted by seconds descending.
- Fixture: tests/test-shard-coverage.sh T5. Executed mutation: replacing the runner's `shardof[idx] = pick` with `idx % 2` turns T5 red (and the empty-shard checks).

## FC-HELP-DRIFT-PLAIN-PROSE-CHANGELOG The help-drift guard went red on main because the release notes stopped using backticks, so it read nothing and called its own extraction broken
- User-visible vs raw: raw (a red gate on a clean main). tests/test-help-changelog-drift.sh failed with "no backticked 'loki ...' invocations in the newest 5 CHANGELOG sections; extraction is broken" on origin/main 9e4812a14 and on 749d5bb46.
- Law: a guard must read the format the writer actually emits; a drift check that reads nothing proves nothing, and it must not be silenced by relaxing the assertion or by adding text to the CHANGELOG.
- Cause (measured): the extractor was correct for its stated contract, but the CHANGELOG sections written since v11.53.0 are plain prose with no backtick at all (`awk '/^## v/{c++} c>=1 && c<=30' CHANGELOG.md | grep -c '`'` gave 0 for the newest 30 sections; the last section with a backtick is v11.52.0). The newest sections do name invocations unbackticked (for example loki doctor --json, loki start --resume), so the writer changed and the reader did not follow.
- Siblings swept: the other tests that read CHANGELOG (test-release-notes.sh, test-p0-verification-sweep.sh, test-enforcement-doc-honesty.sh, test-receipt-signing-discoverability.sh, test-mcp-tool-surface-packaged.sh, test-no-unreachable-shipped.sh) have no backtick-dependent read (grep for CHANGELOG lines mentioning a backtick in them: no output). The assertion text and the 5-section window are unchanged.
- Mechanism: extract_invocations also reads `loki <word> [--flag ...]` from the text left after backticked spans are removed. A plain hit with a --flag is checked exactly like a backticked one. A bare `loki <word>` could be prose, so it is checked only when the word is already a literal command in loki.bash main_commands; measured on the newest 30 sections the plain extractor finds 4 distinct invocations and no prose false positive.
- Fixture: tests/test-help-changelog-drift.sh self-test: plain-prose real flag passes, plain-prose unknown flag is reported by name, bare prose naming no command passes, and a window with no invocation still reports broken extraction. With the plain branch removed the first and third fixtures and the real run go red.

## FC-HELP-DRIFT-WINDOW-CAP The help-drift guard went red on main again because five consecutive slice releases named no command, so its fixed 5-section window held nothing and it called its own extraction broken
- User-visible vs raw: raw (a red gate on a clean main). tests/test-help-changelog-drift.sh exits 1 with "no 'loki ...' invocations (backticked or plain) in the newest 5 CHANGELOG sections; extraction is broken" on origin/main 11ab1df18 (v11.60.6) and on a78f48031 (v11.60.8), after FC-HELP-DRIFT-PLAIN-PROSE-CHANGELOG shipped in v11.60.6.
- Law: the same as FC-HELP-DRIFT-PLAIN-PROSE-CHANGELOG, a guard that reads nothing proves nothing and is not silenced by relaxing the assertion or by adding text to the CHANGELOG; and a window sized in sections is a rate assumption that slice-per-release cadence (3-10 per hour) breaks.
- Cause (measured): sections v11.60.4 to v11.60.8 are plain prose naming no `loki <word>`; the plain-prose extractor is correct, the cap was the fault. The nearest section naming an invocation is within 10 sections (LOKI_HELP_DRIFT_SECTIONS=LOKI_HELP_DRIFT_WINDOW=10 gives a green real run), so a cap of 5 is red today and any larger fixed cap is red again after enough command-free releases.
- Siblings swept: the only other section-count reader of CHANGELOG is the per-version release-notes extraction (scripts/release-notes.sh, tests/test-release-notes.sh), which selects one named version and has no window.
- Mechanism: LOKI_HELP_DRIFT_WINDOW defaults to 1000 (the whole file). The window still widens one section at a time and stops at the nearest section that names an invocation, so only the newest invocation-bearing sections are checked. "Extraction is broken" now means no invocation anywhere in the CHANGELOG. The assertion text is unchanged.
- Fixture: tests/test-help-changelog-drift.sh self-test: an invocation six sections down passes (red at the old cap, green now) and a CHANGELOG with no invocation anywhere still reports broken extraction.

## FC-COMMIT-STAGES-USER-UNTRACKED A commit publishes the user's untracked files that were present before the run (FC-42 family)
- Law: only what the run changed is published; a pre-run user file the run left untouched is never staged into a Loki commit (the same rule dropSet already applies to pre-run tracked dirt).
- Cause (measured, #353 CW-18 test): intake records preexisting_untracked_blobs for every untracked file, but only discardIfSatisfied read it. The commit stage and the supervisor backstop run `git add -A` and dropSet excluded only untouched pre-run tracked dirt, so a normal VERIFIED run committed the user's untracked notes.
- Siblings swept (every site that stages files): commit stage (seal.ts) and backstopCommit (commit_filter.ts, via supervisor) both go through dropSet and are fixed together; attempts.ts, modernize/scheduler.ts and verify.ts stage inside fresh worktrees that hold no user untracked files; check_result.ts and pr_lessons.ts add explicit run-owned paths only; discard.ts already protects them.
- Mechanism: untouchedUntracked (e10ext/preexisting_dirty.ts) returns the intake-untracked files whose bytes and mode still match the snapshot; dropSet resets them from the staged set. A file the run edited stays the run's change. Crash-written files are not in the snapshot (see FC-CRASH-WORK-READ-AS-PREEXISTING).
- Fixture: loki-ts/tests/e10ext/commit_filter_guard.test.ts (commit stage and backstop); red before the change, green after; mutants: ignore the snapshot, treat an edited file as untouched.

## FC-PROVIDER-VERSION-PROBE-FULL-ENV The cline `--version` probe in providers/mcp_config.ts spawned with the full parent env, handing GH_TOKEN, GITHUB_TOKEN and SSH_AUTH_SOCK to an external binary, and main went red on full_env_spawn_guard from 35411094c (CW-10) on
- User-visible vs raw: raw (a credential-exposure boundary and a red guard on main, not a user message).
- Law: no child process receives the ambient GitHub credentials (FC-40); a spawn gets tokenFreeEnv or a listed reason.
- Cause (measured): `clineVersion()` called `spawnSync("cline", ["--version"], { env: process.env })`. The version probe needs no credential, so no allowlist row is justified. tests/util/full_env_spawn_guard.test.ts reported `unlisted: loki-ts/src/providers/mcp_config.ts` alone, in `bun test tests/util` (524 tests, 1 fail) and in the full tests/providers run.
- Order sensitivity: not reproduced. The guard is a pure static scan (walk SRC, regex per call window, compare with the allowlist file); it holds no state, mocks nothing and reads only files, so its result cannot depend on test order. "Passes in some batches" is explained by batches that never include the file (diff-selected shards run it through the walk-all-src marker only when selected). No change to the guard was needed; the behavioral test below fails independently of the guard.
- Siblings swept: every other `process.env` spawn in providers/ is already listed with a reason (claude_flags.ts, runner/providers.ts) or token-free; mcp_config.ts is the only unlisted one (guard output).
- Mechanism: spawn through `tokenFreeEnv(process.env)` (util/safe_git.ts), the shared scoped env builder.
- Fixture: tests/providers/mcp_config.test.ts runs a fake `cline` that records GH_TOKEN, GITHUB_TOKEN and SSH_AUTH_SOCK with canary values set in the parent; red with `env: process.env` (restored in the mutant run), green with tokenFreeEnv. The full_env_spawn_guard goes red on the same mutant.


## FC-WALL-FILE-WITHOUT-RUNNER-SILENTLY-DROPPED A sealed Wall file that no runner of the repo can run was dropped from verify, named nowhere, and the receipt still said wall.passed true
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible. A Wall of `loki_wall_a_test.py` plus `loki_wall_b.rs` (or `.java`, `.rb`) verified with `wall_passed: true` and an empty `not_proven`; the second acceptance test was sealed read-only and never executed.
- Law: a sealed Wall file is either executed and passing, or named NOT PROVEN; a proof never covers a file it did not run.
- Cause (measured): wall.ts guessRunner returns null for any extension other than `.py`, `.go` and the repo's JS/TS runner, so the file was sealed but left out of wallTests and out of not_run_files; verify.ts built wallTests from `map.tests` filtered by the sealed paths, so a path with no test entry vanished, and wall_passed was computed only over the checks that ran.
- Siblings swept: every extension guessRunner refuses (`.rs`, `.java`, `.rb`, `.php`, `.cs`, a JS file with no JS runner detected), a `.py` or JS Wall file the verify test map does not list (same drop in verify), the all-dropped Wall (wall_passed stays unset, never true), publishableWall (requires an executed passing target check, so a dropped file is never published; it is now named in not_proven instead of vanishing), holdout checks (unchanged, they come from a separate store).
- Mechanism: one wording, `NO_RUNNER_REASON` in util/check_result.ts. wall.ts adds each sealed file without a runner to `not_run_files` (seal.ts already turns those into `Wall test not executed: <file>: <reason>`); verify.ts adds the same line to `not_proven` for every sealed path missing from wallTests and refuses `wall_passed: true` while any is missing (a failing check still gives false). Seal's set dedups the identical text.
- Fixture: tests/engine10/verify.test.ts "FC-WALL-FILE-WITHOUT-RUNNER-SILENTLY-DROPPED" (mixed runnable plus `.java` is not a bare true, all-dropped is unset, the runnable-only control stays true with no such line) and the extended tests/engine10/wall.test.ts "a sealed file whose runner cannot be guessed" (the `.rb` is named, the `.py` is not). Mutants: dropping the wall_passed guard, dropping the not_proven push, and dropping the not_run_files push each turn the matching test red.
## FC-REPO-BUNFIG-AND-ENV-REACH-ORCHESTRATOR A bunfig.toml preload or .env in the user's repo ran code in, or set env for, the Loki orchestrator, because bun resolves both from the current directory
- User-visible vs raw: user-visible and a security hole. `loki` runs with the user's repo as cwd, and `bun <cli>` loaded that repo's bunfig.toml (`preload = [...]` is arbitrary code) and `.env` (any variable, LOKI_* and BUN_* included). Measured with real bun 1.3.13: a hostile fixture repo made `loki version` write the preload marker and the .env marker; the same run with `--config=/dev/null --no-env-file` wrote neither (tests/test-bun-entry-no-repo-config.sh, control leg plus route legs).
- Law: no repo file can run code in, or set env for, the Loki orchestrator. The orchestrator's configuration comes from Loki's install and the user's real environment only.
- Cause (measured): every `exec bun "$BUN_CLI"` in bin/loki (9 sites), the first-run completion call and the doctor preflight probe passed the CLI to bun with no config or env flag, so bun's cwd discovery applied.
- Siblings swept in bin/loki: all 9 exec sites (engine10 modernize, --attempts, --spec, start --dry-run, engine10 default, SDK loop, final two generic routes), the `completion --auto` call, and the two doctor preflight probes. The grep gate in the fixture fails on any `"$BUN_CLI"` bun call that bypasses the shared flags.
- Siblings NOT fixed here (outside this slice's file set, each runs bun with the user's repo as cwd, follow-on slices needed): autonomy/loki (2171 `control serve`, 29952 `estimate`, 35491 `exec bun "$cli" control`), autonomy/run.sh (6356 exec-manifest, 27354, 27386, 27407 phase1-hooks), autonomy/lib/cockpit-render.sh (238), autonomy/verify.sh (2949, 2951 engine bundle).
- Mechanism: one flag list `_LOKI_BUN_SAFE_FLAGS=(--config=/dev/null --no-env-file)` and one exec function `_loki_exec_bun <bun> args...` in bin/loki; every route calls it. A project .env is OFF by default (CTO ruling). `LOKI_ALLOW_PROJECT_ENV=1` opts in: bash reads `./.env` itself and exports only plain names, never LOKI_*, BUN_*, NODE_*, LD_*, DYLD_*, PATH, HOME, SHELL, IFS, ENV, BASH_*, and never over a variable already set. `loki doctor` prints a "Project .env" section with a one-line hint when a `.env` exists in the cwd.
- Fixture: tests/test-bun-entry-no-repo-config.sh. New file justification: no existing test owns the concern; test-engine10-dispatch.sh and its siblings assert which route is taken, not which flags bun gets, and none runs real bun against a hostile repo. `git grep -l "exec bun\|BUN_CLI" tests/` lists tests/moat/p9-rule-of-two.sh, tests/test-cp-fresh-install-smoke.sh, tests/test-engine10-dispatch.sh, tests/test-sdk-loop-routing.sh and tests/workspace/99-e2e.sh; the first four only stub or mention bun, and 99-e2e.sh calls the CLI directly. Stubs in test-engine10-dispatch.sh, test-modernize-dispatch.sh, test-bin-loki-bun-resolve.sh and test-d65-routing.sh now drop the two flags before recording argv.
- Mutation check (executed): removing `--no-env-file` from the flag list turns 11 assertions red including "repo .env applied"; removing `--config=/dev/null` turns 10 red including "repo bunfig preload RAN". Against origin/main's bin/loki the fixture is red 15 of 21.
